Measured: 29 rows, $16,177.10, in 3 statements, with no rows lost.

### 7.4 Registry hygiene

The registry validator (`tools/lender_ledger.py` or its operating-registry sibling) checks:
- every operating entity has a source type;
- every payout form and card form compiles, and its entity is typed;
- no operating alias, truncation or form equals a ledger financing alias or an insurer alias;
- `HEALTHCARE_PAYER_ALIASES` collides with no ledger alias and no insurer alias;
- `DEALER_FUNDING_PAYERS` collides with no ledger alias outside the vehicle-financing group, which Q7 names on purpose.

### 7.5 Payer accounts-payable remittance (family 4)

A credit-only verified descriptor, `_PAYER_REMITTANCE_CREDIT_PATTERNS`, at step 12 (method `payer_remittance_descriptor`).

Patterns:
- `\bACC(?:OUN)?TS? PAYABLE\b`
- `^DIRECT DEPOSIT\b(?=.* AP CC$)`
- `\bA ?P (?:PMTS?|PAYMENTS?|PYMTS?)\b`
- `\bCO ENTRY DESCR A ?P\b`
- `\b(?:CCD)?ACC PAY\b`
- `\bVENDOR ?(?:PMTS?|PAYMENTS?|PAY|PYMTS?)(?:SEC)?\b`
- `\bTRADE PAY(?:MENTS?)?(?:SEC)?\b`
- `\bPAYABLES\b`, never on a row that prints `BI ?LL COM` (PAYABLES is Bill.com's payer-side debit wording)
- `\bPO REMIT(?:TANCE)?\b`
- `\bAVID ?PAY\b`, `\bAVIDXCHANGE\b`, `\bCOUPA PAY\b`, `\bPAYMODE\b`
- `\bPAYING BILLS? (?:\d|INV)` (Ramp's form), `\bFOR \d+ BILLS\b`
- `\bBI ?LL COM (?:INV|MULTIPLE INVOICES|RECEIVABLE)\b`, `\b(?:DESCR|DES) BI ?LL COM\b` (receivable forms only), `\bRECEIVABLE\b.*\bBI ?LL COM\b`
- `\bINV(?:OICE)? (?:PMT|PYMT)\b`, `\b(?:OF|FOR) INVOI ?CES?\b`
- `\bRMR IV\b` (X12 invoice remittance)
- the invoice number `\bINV(?:OICE)?S?(?: (?:NO|NUM|NUMBER|N))? ?\d`, only on a row that also prints a payment-channel word: `ACH`, `PMT`, `PAYMENT(S)`, `ZELLE`, `WIRE`, `FEDWIRE`, `CHIPS`, `CCD`, `CTX`, `PPD`, `EFT`, `RTP` or `REMIT`. `DEPOSIT`, `TRANSFER` and `CREDIT` are not channel words here. The number is never read after `CREATED FROM` or `TRANSFER FROM`.
- French: `\bPAIEMENT (?:DE )?FACTURE\b` (structural S56).

Free-text gate: a person-to-person row never verifies here; the channel gate (§6.1 step 9b) closes every consumer rail, whatever the sender prints (Q4). On a `PAYPAL` row and on a wire from a person, the invoice, bill and "for invoices" forms count only when the sender prints a company form. A person's Zelle memo "paying bills" or "Invoice 1234" never verifies.

Veto: it is read on the row after removing the matched words and the credit posting labels (`\b(?:ELECTRONIC|ACH|RTP|ONLINE|WIRE|BOOK|CORP|CORPORATE|ZELLE|MISC|DIRECT|PAYMENT|DEPOSIT|TRANSFER|MOBILE|EFT|CHIPS|FEDWIRE)\s+CREDIT\b|^CREDIT\b`). It refuses:
- financing: `FUND`, `CAPIT`, `CAPTL`, `LOAN`, `LEND`, `FINANC`, `ADVANC`, `MORTG`, `ACCEPTANCE`, `SERVICING`, `SERVICER`, `PORTFOL`, and `CREDIT` with no word boundaries; `CRED(?=ORA|IBLY|\b)`; `CAPI?\b` with any left context; `LEAS(?:E|ES|ING|IN|I)?\b` with any left context; the whole words `CAP`, `FIN`, `FINA`, `FINAN`, `FI`, `FINL`, `FNCL`, `ADV`, `MTG`, `MCA`, `RTR`, `SPV`, `FUN`, `FUNDI`, `LOA`;
- insurance, investment, tax and grants: `INSUR`, `INS`, `ASSURANCE`, `INVEST`, `BROKERAGE`, `SECURITIES`, `WEALT`, `TAX(ES)`, `IRS`, `CRA`, `GRANTS?`, `REBATES?`, `DIVIDENDS?`;
- pay and claims: `PAYROLL`, `SALARY`, `WAGES?`, `REIMB\w*`, `EXPENSES?`, `BONUS`, `COMM?ISS?IONS?`, `CLAIMS?`, `MUTUAL`, `INDEMNITY`, `CASUALTY`, `UNDERWRIT\w*`;
- equipment, floor plan and vehicles: `EQUIP`, `FLOOR`, `MERCH`, `\bVIN\b`;
- SBA: `\bSBAD?\b`, `\bEIDL\b`;
- own money and sweeps: `\bINTERCO(?:MPANY)?\b`, `\bDUE FROM\b`, `\bSHAREHOLDER\b`, `\bOWNERS?\b`, `\bZBA\b`, `\bZERO BAL\w*`, `\bSWEEP\b`, `\bCONTROLLED DISB\w*`, `\b(?:TRANSFER|XFER|TFR) FROM\b`, `\bINTERNAL\b`;
- returns: `\bRTN\b`, `\bRETURN(?:ED)? ITEM\b`, `\bRECALL\w*`, `\bUNPAID\b`, `\bADJ\b`;
- lease-to-own: `ACIMA`.

Where the words appear:
- **Numbered companies (T11):** the rule never verifies a payer that §5.2's numbered-company guard matches: after a template prefix, six or more digits alone (`DIRECT DEPOSIT 7654321 CANA AP CC`); with no prefix, six or more digits and then a province, territory or its cut, or words up to a legal form (`1234567 NOVA SCOTIA LTD DES:VENDOR PAY`).
- **The holder (G3):** the rule never verifies a row the holder test matches (§5.2): `CONTOSO BAKERY LLC DES:VENDOR PAY` into Contoso Bakery's own account stays unverified, and is held as `holder_name` under 5.6.
- **Counter-debit guard (statement pass 6):** a credit decided `payer_remittance_descriptor` goes back to `unverified_deposit` (method `payer_remittance_with_counter_debit`) when a posted debit in the same statement shares the payer's first distinctive word. A distinctive word has four or more letters and is not an AP word, a bank template word, a legal suffix, a word of `GENERIC_WORDS` or a public-payer word of §7.6 (`STATE`, `COUNTY`, `CITY`, `TOWN`, `DEPT`, `DEPARTMENT`, `TREAS`, `GOVERNMENT`). Under 5.6 the row is held as `payer_also_debited` (review required), as the verifier's R2 asked. Measured cost: 19 rows, $242K. Audit fix batch B4 (§20): the guard reads the statement's debits as §8.1's counter-debit holds do (by kind, the bank's receiving fee, a processor's fee beside a settlement and a bill never demote the remittance; a lender debit by whole words and registry entity, never by prefix).

Deferred, and recorded for the owner (§15): government AP grants versus contract fees; foundation and council payers through AP; `TRADE PAY` from trading or securities names; insurers and claims administrators whose names carry no `INS`; development lenders on AP-coded EFTs; property-manager owner distributions to named persons; bare trailing `<payer> AP` entries; EDI PYMNTS from non-financial payers.

Measured as simulated before narrowing: 1,125 rows, $22.94M, 263 statements. After narrowing: about $13.4M. The census restates it at row level.

### 7.6 Government payers (family 5)

A verified rule, `_public_payer_remittance`, at step 13 (method `public_payer_remittance`, source type `government_payer`).

- **Arm A (whole row):** `\bTREAS \d{3}(?: DES)? DOD MISC\b` with no `\bPPD\b`; and `\bTREAS \d{3}\b.*\bMISC PAY(?:SEC)?\b.*\bRMR IV\b`.
- **Arm B:** a public payer **anchored on the payer name** and a payer-side purpose.
  - `payer_name` = `_payee_region(_row_before_receiver(text))` with the bank template words removed: `^(?:ELECTRONIC (?:CREDIT|DEPOSIT)(?: FROM)?|ACH (?:CORP )?CREDIT|ACH DEPOSIT|CCD DEPOSIT|DEPOSIT FROM|PREAUTHORIZED CREDIT|EFT CREDIT)`.
  - The payer pattern must match as `^(?:\S+ ){0,3}(?:<payer>)` on `payer_name`, with no digit and no purpose word before the end of that match. The payer patterns are:
    - `TREAS \d{3}`;
    - `(?:STATE|COUNTY|CITY|TOWN|VILLAGE|TOWNSHIP|BOROUGH|PARISH|DISTRICT|PROVINCE|COMMONWEALTH|GOVERNMENT|GOVT|MUNICIPALITY) OF [A-Z]`;
    - `(?<!\bFROM )(?<!\bTO )(?<!\bTHE )(?<!\bOF )(?<!\bAND )(?<!\bINTO )\b[A-Z]{3,} (?:COUNTY|PARISH|TOWNSHIP|BOROUGH)\b`;
    - `(?:DEPT|DEPARTMENT|MINISTRY) OF`;
    - `DHS|DHHS|DSHS|DCF|DCFS|DSS`;
    - `SCHOOL DIST(?:RICT)?`;
    - `DFAS`.
  - The purpose is read on the row before its receiver fields: `\bVEN(?:DOR)? ?(?:PAY(?:MENTS?)?|P(?:Y)?MTS?)(?:SEC)?\b`, `\bPROV(?:IDER)? ?PAY(?:MENTS?)?(?:SEC)?\b` or `\bEDI ?(?:PYMNTS|PAYMENTS?|PMTS?)(?:SEC)?\b`.
- **Arm C (EBT settlements) is deferred:** one holder in the data, and an open owner question.
- **Payer-name veto** (tax, labour and treasurer agencies): `\b(?:DEPT OF REVENUE|DEPARTMENT OF REVENUE|TAXATION|COMPTROLLER|TREASURER|DEPT OF LABOR|UNEMPLOYMENT|WORKFORCE)\b`. Financing names are vetoed without word boundaries on `payer_name` (`FUNDING`, `FUNDER`, `FINANC`, `CAPITAL`, `LENDING`, `LENDER`), which catches glued lender names.
- **Whole-row veto,** read after deleting Bank of America's boilerplate `ADDITIONAL INFORMATION IS AVAILABLE FOR THIS PMT CONTACT A TREASURY SALES OFFICER FOR ASSISTANCE`:
  - tax, refunds and benefits: `\bTAX(?:ES)?\b`, `\bTAXRFD\b`, `\bTAX ?REF(?:UND)?S?\b`, `\bREFUNDS?\b`, `\bRFD\b`, `\bSTUREFUND\b`, `\bR(?:E)?F(?:U)?ND\b`, a refund glued on the left (`[A-Z]*REFUNDS?\b`, `[A-Z]*RFND\b`: Maryland's `MDREFUND`), `\bEIC\b`, `\b(?:XX)?SOC SEC\b`, `\bSUPP SEC\b`, `\bSSI\b`, `\b(?:XX)?VA BEN\w*\b`, `\bBENEF\w*\b`, `\bBEN\b`;
  - pay and pensions: `\bFED SAL\b`, `\bSALARY\b`, `\bPAYROLL\b`, `\bWAGES?\b`, `\bSTIPENDS?\b`, `\bPER DIEM\b`, `\bRETIRE\w*\b`, `\bPENSION\b`, `\bANNUIT\w*\b`;
  - relief, grants and loans: `\bUNEMPLOY\w*\b`, `\bUI\b`, `\bSTIMULUS\b`, `\bGRANTS?\b`, `\bRELIEF\b`, `\bDISASTER\b`, `\bCARES\b`, `\bPPP\b`, `\bSBAD?\b`, `\bEIDL\b`, `\bEIDG\b`, `\bLOANS?\b`, `\bADVANCES?\b`, `\bADV\b`, `\bFORGIV\w*\b`;
  - family and court: `\bCHI?LD ?SUPP\w*\b`, `\bCHILD ?CTC\b`, `\bCTC\b`, `\bJURY\b`, `\bRESTITUTION\b`, `\bFOSTER\b`, `\bADOPT\w*\b`, `\bKINSHIP\b`, `\bWORK(?:ERS)? ?COMP\b`;
  - claims, awards and recoveries: `\bCLAIMS?\b`, `\bAWARDS?\b`, `\bSUBSID\w*\b`, `\bDISB\w*\b`, `\bPROCEEDS\b`, `\bUNCLAIMED\b`, `\bOVERPA\w*\b`, `\bOVERP(?:Y)?MT\b`, `\bREBATES?\b`, `\bREIMB\w*\b`, `\bBONDS?\b`, `\bESCROW\b`, `\bRETURN(?:ED)?\b`, `\bSTABILI[SZ]\w*`, `\bOFFSET\b`, `\bRECOVERY\b`, `\bARPA\b`, `\bRESCUE\b`, `\bERT?C\b`, `\bINCENTIVES?\b`, `\bTUITION\b`;
  - person-to-person: `\b(?:ZELLE|VENMO|CASH ?APP|E ?TRANSFER|INTERAC)\b`;
  - financing and leases: `\b(?:FUNDING|FUNDER|LENDING|LENDER|FINANC\w*|CAPITAL|CREDIT UNION|FCU|MORTGAGE|LEASE|LEASING|FACTOR\w*)\b`.
  - assistance: `\bASSISTANCE\b`, read only after the boilerplate is deleted (the risk verifier's R2). The evidence verifier's objection was that the boilerplate prints the word; deleting the boilerplate first answers it, and a public payer's "assistance" payment is a program payment, not a sale.
  - Arms A and B also veto `\bSETTLEMENT\b`, and arm B vetoes `\b(?:TRANSFER|XFER|TFR)\b`.
- **Numbered companies and the holder:** arm B never verifies a payer region that §5.2's numbered-company guard or holder test matches.
- **No recurrence gate** (T12). A single public-rail credit verifies.
- **Under 5.6, a public payer's non-sale credit is held.** A credit whose payer region matches a payer pattern above or the payer-name veto, and whose row prints a whole-row veto word (the list above without the person-to-person and financing groups, which other holds cover), is held as `non_sale_purpose` (§8.1). `COMPTROLLER OF MD DES:MDREFUND`, `IRS TREAS 310 DES:TAX EIC` and `STATE OF OHIO DES:GRANT` are held, not counted. Measured on the memo's rows: $0.16M, 407 rows, 39 statements.

A rule-verified row is reviewable (T6), which settles the verifier's "no undo" note. The residual is recorded: Chase can leave the Treasury agency prefix blank, so on arm A's MISC PAY branch, RMR IV is the only real guard.

Measured: 131 rows, $3,870,472.30, in 20 distinct statements. Tryout host: 3 rows, $629,249.40, in 2 statements (dump only). $0-verified statements 1,819 → 1,804. Verified 7.17% → 8.57% on this family alone.

### 7.7 Healthcare payers (family 6)

- The verified pattern becomes `(?:\bH[CE]?|HC)CLAIMPMT(?:SEC)?\b`. Its unbounded branch takes only the full NACHA token, so `THECLAIMPMT` and `CHECLAIMPMT` stay unmatched.
- **Claim first (the insurer-vs-claim order; T18, only on the owner's word).** On credits, a row whose revenue text matches that pattern skips the insurer registry (§6.1 step 5). Inside `_classify_credit_revenue`, the financing, potential-lender and insurer lookups read the text with a space inserted before a glued `HCCLAIMPMT` (a lookbehind on `[A-Z0-9]`), so a 16-character funder name glued to the token is still found. D1, descriptor proceeds, the potential registry, strong non-revenue and reversals still win. The rule is entry-based, so every Blue-plan spelling (`BCBS`, `BCBSM`, `BLUE CROSS BLUE`, Blue Care Network, Anthem Blue Cross, CareFirst) is treated alike. That settles the in-statement split the verifier found.
  - Measured: 177 BCBS rows, $908,159, in 16 statements move from `non_revenue` to `verified_revenue`. 376 alias-escaping Blue rows ($424,597.31, 23 statements) are already in the glue fix.
  - S2 builds claim first behind one switch, `HCCLAIMPMT_CLAIM_FIRST`, whose value is the owner's answer to OQ5. Off, an `HCCLAIMPMT` credit from an insurer-registry payer or a Blue-plan spelling above is held as `insurer_payer` (5.6) and stays unverified (5.5), and the evidence verifier's expectation holds: the glued BCBSM row is not verified. Off also demotes the spaced `BCBSF` and `Blue Cross Blue` claim rows that verify today; that demotion is declared, and the census lists it.
- **`HEALTHCARE_PAYER_ALIASES`** (registry, new): Medicare contractors (Noridian, Palmetto GBA, Novitas, WPS, NGS, CGS, First Coast), state Medicaid programs and Medi-Cal, TRICARE and VA Community Care (`VA CCN`), US health plans that are not in the insurer table (Aetna, Cigna, Humana, UnitedHealthcare, `UHC`, Optum, Molina, WellCare, Centene, Anthem), pharmacy-benefit managers (Express Scripts, Caremark, OptumRx), and claim-payment platforms (Zelis, ECHO Health, InstaMed, VPay, PaySpan).
  - It is read on the payer region, on a row with an electronic-payment form. It is used by Q6 (§8.6).
  - A healthcare payer's credit that prints `\bREIMB\w*`, `\bMEMBER\b`, `\bMBR\b`, `\bSUBSCRIBER\b` or `\bPREMIUM\b` is a member's reimbursement or a premium refund, not a claim paid to a provider: Q6 never verifies it, and it is held as `non_sale_purpose` (§8.1).
  - Canadian benefit carriers (Sun Life, Manulife, Canada Life, Equitable Life, Great-West, Desjardins Insurance, Alberta Blue Cross), RBC's `HEALTH DENTAL CLAIM <carrier>` label (normally a member's reimbursement), NEMT brokers, `CLMEFT`, and a bare X12 `TRN*1*` with no entry description are **out**: they are counted and flagged. They also print broker commissions, member reimbursements or non-claim payments.
- Tests lock today's spaced Chase `Hcclaimpmt Sec:CCD` row. The glued Chase, Huntington `<16-char name>HCCLAIMPMT` and digit-prefixed forms verify. `<name>HCLAIMPMT`, `HCCLAIMPMT0001`, `Hhspaymentsec`, `ASSURANCE OF THECLAIMPMT 123456789 CLAIM 123` and Chase's `CO Entry Descr:Theclaimpmt` stay unverified. A BCBSM glued row verifies with the claim-first switch on, and is held as `insurer_payer` with it off; both are tested. A registered funder's glued row stays `non_revenue` / `financing_proceeds_credit`. An HCCLAIMPMT row with `Merchant Cash Advance` or `Loan Proceeds` stays `non_revenue` / `strong_non_revenue_rule`.

Measured: 1,626 corpus rows, $2,156,058.97, in 34 statements from about 8 healthcare businesses. The largest holds 49% of the dollars, and the 17 statements it lifts from 0% belong to 5 businesses. The tryout host barely moves: 6 rows, $531.

### 7.8 Dealer-only wholesale auto auctions (family 7)

A new operating type, `wholesale_auto_auction`: Manheim (`MANHEIM US`), ACV Auctions (`ACV AUCTIONS`) and OPENLANE (`OPENLANE INC`, `BACKLOTCARS`; not the spaced `BACKLOT CARS`).
- The aliases are read on the originator segment only (`_originator_segment`), in step 10 and in the recurrence pass. An alias in `INDN`, `IND NAME`, a Zelle "to" field or a memo never matches.
- For this type, the single-row context verification and the generic recurrence are off. A branch of the recurrence pass decides alone. It touches only a posted credit with `direction_consistent` true and a revenue method in {`operating_source_requires_context`, `recurring_operating_source`, `operating_source_related_activity`, `operating_source_with_credit_context`}.
- It verifies only an observed payout form:
  - Manheim: `\bMANHEIM US (?:DES )?(?:ACH PAYMNT|EDI PYMNTS)\b`;
  - ACV: `\bACV AUCTIONS INC PAYMENTS\b`, or `\bORIG CO NAME ACV AUCTIONS INC\b.*\bCO ENTRY DESCR PAYMENTS SEC CCD\b`;
  - OPENLANE: `\bORIG CO NAME OPENLANE INC\b.*\bCO ENTRY DESCR OPENLANE SEC CCD\b`, or `^BACKLOTCARS PAYMENT\b`.
  Any other auction row stays `unverified_deposit` (rule `auction_credit_without_payout_form`).
- An ACV credit printing a buyer-payment reference `\bPYMT\d` stays unverified (`auction_credit_prints_buyer_payment_reference`), whatever the date gap.
- A refund pair is found by the broad auction name (`\bMANHEIM\b`; `\bACV AUCTIONS\b` but not `ACV CAPITAL`; `\bOPENLANE\b`, `\bBACKLOT ?CARS\b`), on debits and wire `BNF` lines. It needs an equal amount within 14 days, and each debit is used once. A paired credit stays unverified (`auction_credit_matches_same_source_debit`).
- The $1 floor is absolute. A credit under $1 is never verified and never counts toward recurrence.
- The residual is recorded: an arbitration refund in a later statement, or a partial refund, prints like seller proceeds. The auction risk verifier (R9) found one likely case in the corpus: a small credit whose equal Manheim debit sits in the same account's previous statement. Pairing an auction credit with an equal same-auction debit in the applicant's previous statement would need a package-level pass; it is put to the owner (OQ4) and not built here.

Measured: 138 rows, $282,944.45 (+1.42% of corpus verified). 11 statements leave 0%. Non-revenue unchanged.

### 7.9 Wires that cite an invoice or PO (family 8)

A verified rule, `wire_remittance_purpose`, at step 14.
- **N1, channel.** The normalized row must **begin** with an incoming-wire label: `^(?:WIRE TYPE (?:WIRE|INTL|FX) IN\b|USD WIRE IN\b|[A-Z]{3} WIRE IN\b|FEDWIRE CREDIT\b|CHIPS CREDIT\b|WT FED|WIRE TSF\b|WIRE TRANSFER (?:DEPOSIT|CREDIT|IN|INCOMING|FROM)\b|INCOMING WIRE\b|WIRE IN\b|WIRE FROM\b|(?:DOMESTIC|INTL|INTERNATIONAL|FOREIGN) (?:INCOMING )?WIRE\b|INCOMING FUNDS TRANSFER\b|FUNDS TRANSFER CREDIT TT\b|INBD FUNDS TRANSFER\b|FUNDS TRANSFER WIRE FROM\b)`, or be a Chase `BOOK TRANSFER CREDIT B O` whose originator is not the holder (§7.11). Bank of America's `WIRE TYPE BOOK IN` is read the same way as Chase's book transfer, so the two banks agree. Both book-transfer forms enter only with the owner's yes to T19 (OQ6); until then the wire verifier's N2 stands and they are left out. Audit round 2, R2-B10 (§20): the `WIRE TRANSFER` label reads the plural too (`WIRE TRANSFERS? (?:DEPOSIT|CREDIT|IN|INCOMING|FROM)`, R21 part C), and `INCOMING FEDWIRE`, `INBOUND FEDWIRE`, `INBOUND WIRE` and `INCOMING CHIPS` are N1 labels (R32); `CHIPS` alone is none.
- **Purpose,** read on the row with the party-name fields removed (`\bB O\b…`, `\bN?BNF\b…`, `\bORIG\b(?! TO BNF)…`, `\bACCT \d+\b…`, `\bORIGINATOR NAME\b…`, `\bORG\b…`, and `account_name` when it has 6 or more characters):
  - an invoice word, including one split once by a line wrap (`INVOIC E`);
  - an invoice number `\bINV(?:OICE)?S? ?(?:NO |NUM |NBR |NUMBER )?\d`;
  - a PO `\bPO ?(?:NO |NUM |NBR |NUMBER )?\d{3,}` or `\bPURCHASE ORDERS?\b`;
  - an order number: `ORDER` + number, not after `MONEY`, `STANDING`, `PAYMENT`, `PMT`, `COURT`, `PAY`, `TRANSFER`, `SUPPORT`, `BANK` or `WIRE`;
  - payment for goods; `PAGO PROVEEDOR`, `FACTURA`; SWIFT `REF INV`; `PRO FORMA` (the bare `PI <digits>` form is dropped); `COMPRA DE MERCAD`.
- **Vetoes**, read on the whole row:
  - `_FINANCING_ROOT_PATTERN`, after removing the channel words (audit round 2, R2-B09: R06, §20: U.S. Bank's `WIRE CREDIT REF<n>` and `INCOMING|DOMESTIC|INTL|INTERNATIONAL|FOREIGN WIRE CREDIT` among them, removed from the veto's text only, so the row keeps reading as a wire) and the sending and intermediary bank fields, and also run on the first six words after the originator label with their spaces removed (wrap-tolerant, so `CAPI TAL` and `FUN DING` are caught);
  - the non-sale purposes: `INVEST…`, `DISTRIBUTION`, `DIVIDEND`, `EQUITY`, `MEMBERSHIP INTEREST`, `SHAREHOLDER`, `STOCK`, `CONTRIBUTION`, `PROCEEDS`, `ESCROW`, `CLOSING`, `OVER PAYMENT`, `REBATE`, `RETURN(ED)`, `CREDIT MEMO`, `DUPLICATE`, `SALARY`, `PAYROLL`, `WAGES`, `TAX`, `GRANT`, `DONATION`, `GIFT`, `INTEREST`, `REPAY…`, `PRINCIPAL`, `SECURITY DEPOSIT`, `INTER COMPANY`, `AFFILIATE`, `OWNER(S)`, `PERSONAL`, `COURT`, `JUDGMENT`, `INSURANCE`, `CLAIM(S)`, plus `RETN`, `RTN`, `REJT`, `REJECT(ED)`, `RECALL(ED)`, `UNABLE TO APPLY`, `DRAW(S)`, `DRAWDOWN(S)`, `DISBURS…`, `FEE(S)`, `SCF`, `SUPPLY CHAIN`, `EARLY PAY(MENT)`, `DISCOUNT(ED/ING)`, `PAYOFF`, `SURPLUS`, `EARNEST`, `SUBSCRIPTION(S)`, `COMPENSATION`, `ADJ`, `ADJUSTMENT(S)`, `RMA`, `SHORTAGE(S)`, `ALLOWANCE(S)`, `PROMISSORY`, `CONVERTIBLE`, `BRIDGE`, `SAFE`, `TRANCHE` and `DEPOSIT BACK`;
  - own account: the originator's first two name words equal the beneficiary's, or appear in `account_name`;
  - brand-word affiliate: the originator's first distinctive word equals the first word after `BNF` / `NBNF` or the first word of `account_name`;
  - cut field: an originator that fills its field to within two characters of the width (Santander 16, Bank of America 25) whose run-together name starts any financing or potential alias or ledger entity of 6+ characters, or whose last word of 3+ letters is a proper prefix of a financing word (`FUNDING`, `FINANCIAL`, `CAPITAL`, `LENDING`, `LOANS`, `ADVANCE`, `MERCHANT`, `CREDIT`, `LEASING`, `SERVICING`, `FACTORING`, `MORTGAGE`, `RECEIVABLES`, `EQUIPMENT`, `ACCEPTANCE`, `COLLECTIONS`, `RECOVERY`, `SETTLEMENT`, `REMITTANCE`, `EQUITIES`, `EQUITY`, `VENTURES`);
  - `match_known_payee(text)` is not None.
- **Stated:** tuition wires that cite an invoice verify (the invoice is the proof, and the holder is a school). A wire from a private person that cites an invoice verifies (about 4 rows, about $119K), as proposed. Requiring an organisation cue would cost $3.28M of $4.15M.

Measured with N1 to N8: 75 rows, $4,093,290.86, in 27 statements; non-revenue unchanged. The registry side findings go to the ledger, not this rule: eCapital and Stenn in `FACTORING_ALIASES`, the printed legal names of FairFigure, GFE, One River Group and Uncapped, and the spaced `SAMSON SERVICING`.

### 7.10 Property-management platforms (family 9)

Buildium, AppFolio and Landlord Studio join the registry as type `property_management_payments` (T15). `YARDI` stays out, and `FUNDING` is never a context word.
- For this type, step 10 returns `unverified_deposit` / `operating_source_requires_context` even when the context patterns match. Relay's `ACH Credit:`, PNC's `ACH Credit` and ATB's `Direct Deposit Misc. Payments` print those words on every credit.
- The recurrence pass counts recurrence only: three or more credits, or two in a business-named account. It uses no related fee debit. It pools only the platform's ACH payout shape (a row with an electronic-payment form, §5.2), never a push or real-time shape (`VISA DIRECT`, `PMNT RCVD`, `PAYMENT RECEIVED`, `RTP`), so one Visa Direct row plus one PMNT RCVD row never verify each other (property evidence verifier R4). A push-shaped platform row stays `operating_source_requires_context`.
- A guard runs only when one of the three matched. A row printing `\bOWNERS? ?(?:DIST\w*|DISB\w*|DRAWS?\w*|CONT\w*)`, `\bDISTRIBUT\w*`, `\bSEC(?:URITY)? ?DEP\w*`, `\bDEP(?:OSIT)? ?(?:RETURN\w*|RTN\w*|RT\w*|REFUND\w*)`, `\bSD ?(?:RETURN\w*|RTN\w*|RT\w*|REFUND\w*)`, `\bADVANCE\w*` or `\bORIG CO NAME\b.*\bORIG CO NAME\b` stays unverified (`property_platform_owner_or_deposit_wording`) and is left out of the count.
- Micro-deposits never count: `\bACCT ?VERIFY\w*`, `\bMICRO ?(?:DEP\w*|ENTRY)\b`, `\bPENNY TEST\b`, `\bTRIAL (?:DEP\w*|CREDIT)\b`, or under $1.
- A verified row carries the context signal `property_management_collections`, shown as a chip (§10.6).

Measured: 78 rows, $263,112.59, in 8 statement-months of 3 bank accounts; 0 tryout-host rows. The AppFolio rows are payouts to a landlord (owner distribution or rent), not vendor payments.

### 7.11 Wrong non-revenue, and newly named non-revenue

Narrowings of non-revenue rules (§6.3):
- **REIMBURSEMENT.** `\bREIMBURSEMENT\b` in `_REVERSAL_CANCELLATION_CREDIT_PATTERNS` does not fire only when the row prints a claim or provider signal: `MEDICAID`, `MEDICARE`, `MEDI CAL`, `TRICARE`, `CHAMPVA`, `GEMT`, `EMS`, `HCCLAIMPMT` (§7.7's pattern), `PROV PAY` (§7.6's provider-pay pattern), `EDI PYMNTS` or `CLAIM PMT`. A healthcare or government payer's name alone is not enough: `UNITEDHEALTHCARE DES:REIMBURSEMENT` (a member's reimbursement) and `STATE OF TEXAS DES:TRAVEL REIMBURSEMENT` (an employee's travel) stay `non_revenue`. An employer's `EXPENSE REIMBURSEMENT` stays `non_revenue`. The abbreviated `EXP REIMB` does not match the whole word, so it is not `non_revenue` today; under 5.6 it is held as `payroll_or_salary` (§8.1).
- **Street `LN`** (`analysis/description_text.py:24`). `\bLN\b` is rewritten to `LOAN` except when it ends a street address: a token of digits followed by one to three alphabetic tokens, then `LN`. The guard never applies when a loan-product word sits among those tokens (`COMMERCIAL`, `COMM`, `AUTO`, `BUSINESS`, `BUS`, `TERM`, `PERSONAL`, `MORTGAGE`, `MTG`, `EQUIPMENT`, `EQUIP`, `INSTALLMENT`, `INSTL`, `CONSUMER`, `HOME`, `HELOC`, `STUDENT`, `VEHICLE`, `SBA`, `BRIDGE`, `CONSTRUCTION`, `LINE`), or when `LN` is followed by `PMT`, `PYMT`, `PAYMENT`, `DISB`, `DISBURSEMENT`, `ADV`, `ADVANCE`, `PROCEEDS` or `DRAW`, so `1234 COMMERCIAL LN PMT` and `000123 AUTO LN DISB` still read LOAN. The same guard applies to printed Wells Fargo and Chase ATM and branch locations. This is the only normaliser change. It moves `normalized_description` for such rows, debits and credits, so G7 (§14.3) must show no lender or flag moved on debits, and G7b must list every credit whose revenue decision it moved. Measured: 8 or 9 rows, $19K to $32K, including one $105K wire.
- **Book and funds transfers.** In `_INTERNAL_TRANSFER_CREDIT_PATTERNS`:
  - `BOOK TRANSFER` and `FUNDS TRANSFER` stay own-account only when no third-party originator is printed (`B O`, `CUSTOMER`, `ORIGINATOR`, `ORG`, `TT <payer>`, `FROM <payer>`, `WIRE FROM`), or when that originator matches the holder's name (§5.2's holder test). The `BOOK TRANSFER` half ships only with the owner's yes to T19 (OQ6); the `FUNDS TRANSFER` half is this spec's decision. Audit round 2, R2-B10 (R11, §20; Q5 outranks that decision): a row an incoming-wire label starts that prints only a reference (RBC's `FUNDS TRANSFER CREDIT TT <reference> [O]` or `TT RBC<reference>`, `INCOMING FUNDS TRANSFER <reference>`), no originator, no account word and no own-account product word after the label, is an incoming wire whose sender is not printed: `wire_no_sender` (held for review, so the review gate reads it), never own-account. An account or product word after the label (`TT SAVINGS <n>`, `FROM BUSINESS SAVINGS`, `LOAN PROCEEDS`), the holder named as the originator, a funds or book transfer no incoming-wire label starts, and the EFT channel keep this bullet's reading. R2-B10 review round 1: "only a reference" is read word by word, so every word after the label is a reference (digits, a masked token, a month), a single letter (RBC's `O` or `W`) or a wire label word (§8.5's one label list, and `TT` and `FROM`); any other word (`<n> BUSINESS`, `<n> TRUST`, `<n> USD`, `USD <n>`, `<n> PAYMENT`, `<n> ONLINE`, `<n> INC`, `<n> THE`) is no reference and keeps this bullet's own-account reading, never a counted wire whose "sender" is that word; and pass 7 reads no sender on a row that prints only a reference, whatever word of it the payer reader keeps (a month, a stray `TT` or `FROM`).
  - `INCOMING FUNDS TRANSFER`, `FUNDS TRANSFER CREDIT TT`, `INBD FUNDS TRANSFER` and `FUNDS TRANSFER WIRE FROM` are incoming-wire labels. `ELECTRONIC FUNDS TRANSFER DEPOSIT` is an EFT channel, so the registry reads the processor that follows.
  - `prints_internal_transfer_designation` reads the same tuple, so R-d's package pairing narrows with it. A book transfer from a third party designates no own account, which is R-d's own test; nor does the bare incoming wire of the bullet above (audit round 2, R2-B10: R11).
  - Chase's Paymentech settlements delivered as a book transfer (`B O CUSTOMER PAYMENTECH LLC … DEPOSIT ID`) then reach the registry.
  - Measured: 308 rows, $6.25M, in 73 statements (book transfers); 119 rows, $2.27M, in 26 statements (funds-transfer wires).
- **Push payouts printed as card credits.** `DEBIT CARD CREDIT` and `POS CREDIT` do not fire when the row prints a push-rail word (`VISA DIRECT`, `MC SEND`, `VISA MONEY TRANSFER`, `FASTPAY`, `INSTANT PAY`, `INSTANT PAYOUT`), or names a `merchant_processor` or `invoice_and_receivables` source that also prints a payout word (`\b(?:PAYOUTS?|INSTANT (?:PAYOUT|PAY|TRANSFER)|SETTLEMENT|DEPOSIT)\b`). Naming any other maintained source is not enough: Uber, DoorDash, PayPal, Instacart, Toast, Etsy, Shopify and Amazon also print their names on a customer's card refund (`DEBIT CARD CREDIT UBER *TRIP HELP.UBER.COM CA`). A released row:
  - is verified only by a form or by step 10's single-row context (`DEBIT CARD CREDIT VISA DIRECT SQUARE INC` verifies through Square's real-time-rail context);
  - otherwise stays `unverified_deposit` and is held as `possible_return` under 5.6;
  - never joins the recurrence pool (§7.0's veto), so three refunds from one source never verify each other.
  A retail merchant's card refund keeps the reversal. The first draft measured 126 rows, $220K, in 12 statements on the wider release; the census restates the narrower one. Audit fix batch B3 (§20; amends the final review's M5 line): with the owner's switch on, a card credit whose whole payer is a merchant processor is that processor's payout when the statement prints the processor's ACH payout or pull and no card purchase at it (§6.3 narrowing 9).
- **Insurer customer-payment wording** (T14): §6.1 step 5. The row leaves `non_revenue` but stays out of True.
- **Q7:** §8.7 (5.6 carries it with the definition, since the owner ruled it as one).

Tightened verification (a declared verified → unverified move):
- `CUSTOMER` in `_VERIFIED_REVENUE_CREDIT_PATTERNS` becomes `\bCUSTOMER\b(?! ID\b)`, and neither CUSTOMER pattern accepts `CHECK` or `CHEQUE` (Q3). Truist prints `CUSTOMER ID` on every ACH line. Measured: 5 verified keys, $125K.

Newly named non-revenue (T5):
- the return codes, added to the credit markers only (the debit rules keep `_REFUND_REVERSAL_PATTERNS`): `\bRTN\b`, `\bRETN\b`, `\bRFND\b`, `\bREFND\b`, `\bRFD\b`, `\bRVSL\b`, `\bRVRSL\b`, `\bREVSL\b`, `\bRECALL(?:ED)?\b`, `\bUNPAID\b`, `\bREJT\b`, `\bCHBK\b`, `\bCHGBK\b`. The reversal-pair pass reads them too, which closes the returned-wire hole the wire verifier found. A won chargeback printed `CHBK REVSL` becomes `non_revenue`, in the conservative direction.
- tax refunds: `\bTAX ?REF(?:UND)?S?\b`, `\bTAXRFD\b`, `\bTAX RFD\b` join the TAX REFUND rule (Treasury's `IRS TREAS 310 TAX REF`);
- D1 by descriptor, credits only: `square_capital_credit` for `\bSQ ?CAP\d*\b` on a row that names Square; `sba_disbursement_credit` for `\bSBAD TREAS\b`, `\bSBA (?:EIDL|LOAN|DISB\w*)\b` and `\bEIDL\b`. Both are financing proceeds, subtracted from eligible deposits;
- account verification: `\bACCT ?VERIFY\w*\b`, `\bMICRO ?DEPOSITS?\b`, `\bPENNY TEST\b`, `\bTRIAL DEPOSITS?\b` join the strong non-revenue rule.
- audit round 2, R2-B08 (§20): the French interest credit (`CREDIT D INTERET`, `CREDIT DINTERET`, `INTERET CREDITE`) and fee rebate (`RABAIS [SUR] [LES] FRAIS`) join the strong non-revenue rule beside `INTEREST CREDIT` and `FEE REBATE`.

A carrier's bare `PAYMENTS` entry stays `non_revenue`: a business's own claim prints the same way (Q6: insurers never count) |

S25 (won chargebacks, reserve releases) is out; a reserve release under the `bankcard` form stays sales. S36 (consumer-finance funders) is out; they are counted. S54 (a holder named "… LOANS" makes every credit non-revenue) is out; it errs toward non-revenue, and one corpus row is affected. S55 (program reimbursements, tax-prep fee disbursements) is out; it waits on the subsidy ruling. S59 (lockbox) is out for Verified; it is counted as `check_deposit` (Q3). S60 is covered by Q7.

## 8. The definitional rules (classification 5.6)

Statement pass 7 (§6.2) runs after every verifying rule. It first computes each posted credit's payer facts (channel family, payer region, payer name, payer key, entry fields, and the hold predicates of §8.1). Then it runs Q7's credit-union arm (§8.7) and the Q5 and Q6 verifications (§8.5, §8.6), which refuse any row a hold of §8.1 names. Last, it gives every remaining `unverified_deposit` its tier and reason, in the order of §8.10.

### 8.1 Holds that apply to any channel

Every hold below skips a row that the registry, a payout entity or a factoring payee claimed (§8.9), except `insurer_payer`, `possible_return` and the pay and benefit holds, which read wording no platform prints on its own payouts. The **posting labels** removed before a hold reads a row are §7.5's credit posting labels, plus `INSTANT CREDIT`, `REAL TIME CREDIT`, `COUNTER CREDIT`, `POS CREDIT`, `ATM CREDIT`, `TELLER CREDIT`, `BRANCH CREDIT`, `CARD CREDIT`, `BATCH CREDIT`, `MERCHANT CREDIT` and `CREDIT FROM`, and the channel phrases `FUNDS TRANSFER`, `FUNDS AVAILABLE` and `INSTANT FUNDS`.

- **`insurer_payer`:** a row whose revenue method is `insurer_customer_payment` (§6.1 step 5, T14). The customer-payment wording, read on a credit from a listed insurer, is `\b(?:DRP|VENDOR ?(?:PMT|PAY|PAYMENT)|COMM?ISS?IONS?|AGT ?COMM|AGENT COMP|AGTEFT|REPAIR)\b`.
- **`insurer_named_payer`** (new): the payer region prints `\bINSUR\w*`, `\bASSURANCE\b`, `\bMUTUAL\b`, `\bCASUALTY\b`, `\bINDEMNITY\b` or `\bUNDERWRIT\w*`, or `\bCLAIMS?\b` on a row that is neither an `HCCLAIMPMT` entry nor from a healthcare payer. It closes Q6's "insurers never count" for carriers no registry lists (`FABRIKAM MUTUAL INSURANCE COMPANY DES:CLAIM PAYMT`). Measured on the memo's rows: $1.06M, 527 rows, 61 statements.
- **`possible_return`:**
  - `\bRETURN(?:ED|S)?\b`, `\bADJ(?:USTMENT|USTED)?\b`, `\bDISPUTE[DS]?\b`, `\bCREDIT MEMO\b`, `\bOVERPA\w*`, on a row the reversal rule did not claim;
  - a `DEBIT CARD CREDIT` or `POS CREDIT` row released from the reversal rule (§7.11) that no form and no single-row registry context verified;
  - audit fix batch B3 (§20): a card credit printed first (`CARD CREDIT`, `CARD CREDIT <merchant>`) with no push or person-to-person rail, and, as a backstop, step 3's 5.6 return, correction and refund spellings.
- **`financing_named_payer`** (T4). The financing roots are read, after the posting labels are removed, on:
  - the payer region (§5.2), read before D11's filtering, so `CAPITAL ONE …` keeps `CAPITAL`;
  - the entry fields (§5.2): `DES`, `CO ENTRY DESCR`, a wire's `OBI` and purpose text, and a memo;
  - on a row with no payer region (a check, cash, branch, ATM or bare row), the row before its receiver fields.

  A run that equals the holder's whole normalized `account_name` (two or more words) is removed first, so a label-less receiver field never supplies a root; audit fix batch B7 (I47, §20): so is a run of its first two words or more, with the legal form printed or left off, when the account name names the holder (a company form or a person's name, never a product name such as `BUSINESS REWARDS CHECKING`). Audit round 2, R2-B04 (R19, R40, §20): the holder's name names the holder also with a trailing `CO` after two words or more or Quebec's `S.E.N.C.` (a city and state such as `SAMPLEVILLE CO` still names nobody), when a side of a `DBA`, `AKA` or BMO's `Operating as:` name names it, and with a leading `THE` or a partnership's `AND` (or `&`) read as no word of the name (a name of five words or more with no form still names nobody); the runs of each such side are removed too, and a run's last word of five letters or more cut by the bank's field to four letters or more at the row's end (`NORTHWIND DISTRIBUTIO`, `NORTHWIND DISTRIB`); and so is every business name of the holder's, never a person's: beside the holder's name, the account-name field's business when the holder hold reads it as a second name (a parser's holder name `JOHN SAMPLE` beside the account name `NORTHWIND CAPITAL LLC`) and reads as no person's name, and the trade side of a `DBA` name always, its legal side when it prints a company form (`JOHN SAMPLE DBA NORTHWIND PAYROLL SERVICES` adds `NORTHWIND PAYROLL SERVICES`, whose `SERVICES` keeps the side from naming a holder by itself); a person-shaped account-name field beside the parser's holder name (an owner's or a co-owner's name) is never added, so that person's pay from another company stays held (Q8). A whole-word `CREDIT` counts as a root only inside a payer name, next to another name word (`UNITED AUTO CREDIT`, `CREDIT ACCEPTANCE`, `FABRIKAM CREDIT UNION`); in an entry field and on a row with no payer region, a standalone `CREDIT` is a posting word and is never read (BofA's `COUNTER CREDIT`, `DES:CREDIT`). Glued, it always counts (`BIZ2CREDIT`, `CREDITLINE`). Audit fix batch B7 (I16, §20): the plural `CREDITS` is a statement's section word (`DEPOSITS AND CREDITS`, `OTHER CREDITS`), never a root, whole or glued, except (B7 review round 1, §20) before a company form after another name word in a payer region (`<NAME> CREDITS LLC`), where it is a lender's name word as `CREDIT` is; and a whole-word `CREDIT` that ends the payer region after a single letter is the entry word a layout prints after an originator name its field cut (`<NAME> P CREDIT`).

  Audit fix batch B7 (I16, §20; the roots are read only on text that can name the payer): a cash, branch, ATM or check deposit with no payer region prints its location (`… DEPOSIT 123 GLENDALE AZ`, `… CAPITAL BLVD …`, `ATM DEPOSIT <NAME> FIN CU <street>`), so no root is read on its location (Q2, Q3): B7 review round 1 (§20), its branch's label (`<NAME> FIN CTR`, `… FINANCIAL CENTER`), its credit union's name (`<NAME> FIN CU`, `… CREDIT UNION`, `… FCU`) and a street's name are removed, and what is left is read for whole financing words only (a word that starts with a financing word, `CREDIT` aside, or a whole-word root; never a root inside a word, such as a town's name), so a funder's name printed on the deposit (`MOBILE DEPOSIT <NAME> FUNDING LLC`, `COUNTER CREDIT <NAME> MERCHANT FUNDING`) still holds; B7 review round 2 (§20): the deposit's bank (the statement's own, a listed sending bank), a place a suffix names (`PLAZA`, `MALL`, `CIR`, `CIRCLE`, `CTR`, `CENTER`, `CENTRE`, `SQ`, `SQUARE`, `DISTRICT`, `HEIGHTS`, `PARK`), up to two words before `BRANCH`, and a town of one or two words before a state's code that ends the row or comes before a ZIP, a reference, a country or `ON <date>` (never a company form; `CO` is a company form there, never Colorado's code) are its location too, and a buyer's advance purchase or payment (`ADVANCE TICKET SALES`, `ADVANCE PAYMENT`) and a hold notice's `FUNDS` or `FUNDED` (`FUNDS ON HOLD`, `FUNDS AVAIL NEXT DAY`; still read before a company form, `<NAME> FUNDS LLC`) are never read, so `ATM DEPOSIT <n> ADVANCE NC`, `… CAPITAL HEIGHTS MD`, `1 FINANCIAL PLAZA`, `COUNTER CREDIT CAPITAL CITY BRANCH` and a Capital One ATM on a Capital One statement are counted; on every row a bank's branch label after a bank's name (`BOFA FIN CTR`, `… FINANCIAL CENTER`), the banks' page furniture (`MEMBER FDIC`, `EQUAL HOUSING LENDER`), a fundraising purpose (`FUNDRAIS…`) and Wave Payments' payout entry (`WAVE <code> FUNDIN WAVE`, `WAVE FUNDING INSTRU…`) are removed first; on a person-to-person rail row the sender's bank (a listed sending bank, the statement's own bank, a credit union's name before `CREDIT UNION` or `FCU`) is removed, and a sender that reads as a person's name (two to five words of letters, no company form, no holder-noise, business or institution word, no whole-word root and no word starting with a financing word: `TRUE FINANCE`, `FAST FUNDING` stay read) is not read, so a root inside a surname holds nothing (Q4); on a wire's purpose text a buyer's `ADVANCE PAYMENT`, `ADVANCE PMT` or `PREPAYMENT` for goods is removed (Q5). A memo that says funding (`ZELLE … FOR FUNDING`), a sender whose own name carries a root, and an ACH entry's `ADVANCE PMT` still hold. Customers whose own names carry a root (`ADVANCED …`, `… FINANCIAL`), a lessor's `LEASE` rent and lenders paying for services wait for the owner's ruling on audit I16. Audit round 2, R2-B09 (R06, §20): a layout's posting or channel label that prints `CREDIT` (`EDI EFT CCD CREDIT`, `INTERNET CREDIT`, `PREAUTH CREDIT`, `HIP CREDIT RCVD FROM`, `AVIS DE CREDIT`, `AUTOMATED CREDIT`) is a template prefix (§5.2), so it is never in the payer region and never a lender's name word; nor is the ACH entry `CREDIT NONE` ending the payer region after a cut name (`<NAME> CREDIT NONE`, as `<NAME> P CREDIT`), nor a credit union's `DIVIDEND CREDIT` (a dividend: held as `non_sale_purpose`). A lender named after the label (`PREAUTH CREDIT <NAME> ADVANCE LLC`, `ACH CREDIT <NAME> CREDIT CORP`), a credit union's own cut name as the originator (I16, with the owner) and Capital One's name stay held.

  The roots: `(?:FUND|CAPIT|CAPTL|LOAN|LEND|FINANC|ADVANC|MORTG|ACCEPTANCE|SERVICING|SERVICER|LEASING|PORTFOL)`, `CRED(?=ORA|IBLY)`, `CREDIT` glued on either side, or the whole words `CAP`, `CAPI`, `FIN`, `FINA`, `FINAN`, `FINL`, `FNCL`, `ADV`, `MTG`, `MCA`, `RTR`, `SPE`, `SPV`, `LEASE` (audit fix batch B6, §20: `SPE`, a special-purpose entity). `FACTOR` is not a root: a factor's payout is revenue (owner, 2026-09-26), and registered factors are decided before this pass. A glued `FINUSA` is not a root (B6 review round 1, §20): with no left boundary it read any run-together payer name ending in `FIN` + `USA` (a muffin or griffin brand, `<NAME>FINUSA LLC`), held even as a Q6 repeat; Forward Financing's run-together `FORWARDFINUSA` is its ledger alias, read with a reference glued on (§6.1 step 6).

  Recorded residuals of the roots, for the owner's T4 ruling (audit I16, I36(d): T4 holds any payer whose name carries a root, invoice-paying customers named `ADVANCED…` or `…FINANCIAL` among them):
  - a real-estate owner entity named a special-purpose entity (`<ADDRESS> OWNER SPE LLC`, as an `SPV` already was) that pays a contractor or a property manager by ACH is held, even as a Q6 repeat; a wire with an invoice or PO stays Verified (the holds skip a descriptor-verified row). In the corpus the whole word `SPE` moved only the three vehicle wires of §20's B6 line; its other rows were already the registry's financing proceeds or held;
  - Forward's run-together spelling with a letter after its reference (`FORWARDFINUSA<n><letter>`) matched no alias and carried no root, so it was counted, even beside Forward's debits (`payer_debited_as_lender` splits off only a trailing digit run; `FORWARDFINUSA<letter>` with no digit is still held there). Closed by audit round 2, R2-B08 (R47 (b), §20; Q6 "lenders never count", standard C1): the credit index (never the debit index, the premium-finance lookup or the multi-word glued arm) also reads a whole run-together alias with a digit run and one or two letters glued after it (`FORWARDFINUSA1X`, `FORWARDFINUSA12AB`), the reference-tied rule applying, so such a credit is Forward's financing proceeds; a tail of three letters or more is a word and is still counted (a documented limit). No cached input prints one: every `FINUSA` token of the 7,241 cached inputs is `FORWARDFINUSA` with a digit run ending it, on a credit.

  Also held here:
  - audit fix batch B6 (I23, §20): a bank that lends to small businesses on its own account or as a fintech's lender of record (the registry's `LENDING_BANK_CREDIT_PAYERS`: Celtic Bank, Newtek Bank; audit round 2, R2-B03: R05, and Northeast Bank, the national SBA lender the ledger lists by its repayment spellings only), named in the payer region (on a row with none, in the row before its receiver fields); credits only, never a debit alias;
  - a credit from a `DEALER_FUNDING_PAYERS` lender that the ledger has not yet admitted, and that Q7 did not verify (§8.7);
  - a row that prints a Q7 dealer-funding word (`VHCL PURCH`, `VEHICLE PURCHASE`, `DEAL FUND`, `CONTRACT FUND`, `DEALER FUND`, `DLR PMT`, `AUTODLRPMT`, `VADMIN FEE`, `FUNDLOAN`) that Q7 did not verify; audit fix batch B7 (I36, §20): never a wire whose purpose alone prints the word and whose sender is a named non-lender (no root, no floor-plan lender's name (B7 review round 1, §20; B7 review round 2, §20: a floor-plan form, `FLOOR PLAN…` or `FLOORPLAN…`, or `WHOLESALE…` beside a lender's or a vehicle trade's word, `FUND…`, `FIN…`, `LEND…`, `CAP…`, `CREDIT…`, `AUTO…`, `MOTOR…` or `DEALER…`; never a flooring contractor's or a wholesale grocer's name), no credit union's or bank's form, no financing payee of the registry, no potential lender): a customer's own wire to the dealer (`B/O <person> … OBI VEHICLE PURCHASE`) is Q5's. An unlisted originator's ACH entry (`<NAME> AUTO DES:DEALER PAY`) is still held.

  Measured on the memo's rows with the rule as written here (`work/spec/revision/revision_probe.py`): $2.1M, 402 rows, 174 statements. The first draft's $5.0M read the whole row before the receiver fields, and left out the branch rows its wording would also have held (`COUNTER CREDIT`: 307 rows, $4.92M, as the review measured).
- **`payer_debited_as_lender`:** the payer's first distinctive word (§7.5) is also the first distinctive word of a posted debit in the same statement whose state is `confirmed_lender`, `potential_lender` or a financing group. This is the unregistered funder that funds and then collects. Audit fix batch B4 (§20: I22, I38): the payer and the debit also match when both name one registry lender entity, when a name is glued on one side and spaced on the other, when a reference glued to the name is split off (`<NAME>USA1`), and, for an unverified credit (one no rule verified: like the first-word match, this hold comes before Q5 and Q6, so a glued funder name holds a company-form or repeat payer as the spaced name does), when one first word of five letters or more starts the other and the longer runs on into a financing root or into the shorter side's next word; two different registry entities never match except by the first word itself. Audit fix batch B6 (§20: I49): the credit's words are read past an instant-payment rail label (PNC's `INSTPMNTIN` before the payer region, and on a row whose payer region is empty the RTP network's `RTP CREDIT`, never `RTP CREDIT FROM`), for this hold and `payer_also_debited`. Audit round 2, R2-B02 (R02, §20), for this hold and `payer_also_debited`: a wallet's cash-out names no sender (§5.2); the words that name no counterparty are never the word a payer or a debit is read by (the first distinctive word and the B4 forms): the person-to-person rails' own words (`ZELLE`, `INTERAC`, `VENMO`, `POPMONEY`, `AUTODEPOSIT`, `CASH APP`), RBC's French label `PAIEMENT`, the US states (each word of a two-word state that is no generic word or name-starter), the provinces and territories, `CANADA`, `CANADIAN` and `AMERICAN`, so `Paiement divers <customer>` is never held beside `Paiement divers <lender>` and a Zelle debit is read by its recipient; and a person-to-person or push credit whose sender prints a name of two words or more (read up to its legal form) and no company form matches a debit in `payer_also_debited` on that whole name, its first two words glued (`QUILLANSAMPLE`), or, on a debit for its own amount that is no person-to-person or push debit to a named recipient, on its first word as B4 read it, so a given name alone never holds a customer and the same person's Zelle or e-Transfer round trip is held; a sender printing a company form is compared by the B4 forms as before. In this hold the whole name is one more arm, never the only one (R2-B02 review round 1, §20): the first word, the B4 forms and the prefix still read such a credit, so a funder printing no legal form that pushes by a real-time or Zelle rail and pulls under a glued or one-word name is still held. RBC's `Paiement divers COMPTE CHEQUE <n>` or `COMPTE EPARGNE <n>` (the holder's own chequing or savings account), and (review round 1) a credit from an account designation printed with no transfer word (Bank of America's banking-centre `BKOFAMERICA BC <date> <ref> FR SAV` or `FR CHKG`: `FR` before `SAV`, `SAVINGS`, `SVGS`, `CHKG`, `CHK`, `CKG`, `CHECKING`, `DDA` or `MMA`), are held as `transfer_account_designation` (T13), before this hold.
- **`holder_name`:** the holder test of §5.2 matches the payer region. It never runs on a check, cash, branch, ATM or bare row. Audit fix batch B9 (I13, §20): nor on a processor's or a platform's settlement or payout under the merchant's own name (`NET SETTLE`, `NETSWEEP`, `FINTECHEFT`, `BANKCARD`, `MERCH`, `MERCHANT`, `MERCHPAYOUT`, `MRCHNTSRVS`), which prints the holder as its originator and its receiver. Audit fix batch B9 review round 1 (I13, §20): those words are read outside the holder's own names (the parser's holder name, a second name in the account-name field, each side of a DBA name, the receiver-field holder names; for the row-alone test below, the row's own receiver and originator), never on an incoming wire or a person-to-person or push rail, which print no processor's settlement: a holder named `NORTHWIND MERCHANT GROUP LLC` keeps its own Zelle, push and wire held, while `NETSWEEP` after its name is still the processor's. And a row moves the holder's own money in on its face, whatever names the statement prints, when it is an incoming wire whose sender is its beneficiary (the first two words of each, the own-account reading §7.9's wire veto refuses Q5 with; audit fix batch B9 review round 1, §20: and the two whole names one by the class-B reading below, each read up to its first digit token and cut after its first legal form, so `THE SAMPLE COMPANY INC` wiring `THE SAMPLE BAKERY LLC` is two companies, counted and refused as proof; review round 2: a name no legal form ends is read without the town and state its address prints after it, one town word before a state's code, a country code after the state included, when two name words stay, so the holder's own `NORTHWIND MARINE SAMPLEVILLE TX` wiring `NORTHWIND MARINE OTHERTOWN TX` is held while `NORTHWIND MARINE SUPPLY` wiring `NORTHWIND MARINE REPAIR` is two), or an ACH or EFT credit whose originator is the row's own receiver field (`INDN`, `IND NAME`, `RECEIVER NAME`, `RECV NAME`; Capital One's unlabelled receiver after the originator's entry and digit run): the two names equal, or one starting the other at a word with two words or more, or as a cut of fifteen characters or more, legal forms aside (the census's class B reading). One shared first word is not one name. Such a row is held as `holder_name` too. A platform's payout known by its payout id (audit round 2, R2-B02: R18, §20; amends B9's reading, an entry field leading with the originator's own first word, which the holder's own push printing its name passed too): Bank of America's `ID:ST-<id>` and Chase's `IND ID:ST-<id>`, ten characters or more holding letters and digits (R2-B02 review round 1: a town after an ID label, `ID:ST <town>`, is none), on a row that is no wire and no person-to-person or push rail, is no own push and is never held by the holder test above or read by the census's holder fact (G3), whatever descriptor it prints as its originator and entry (`<descriptor> DES:<descriptor> ID:ST-<id> INDN:<merchant>`); it lifts the holds only and never verifies. The holder's own push whose entry echoes its name (`NORTHWIND MARINE DES:NORTHWIND ID:<digits> INDN:NORTHWIND MARINE LLC`) is held on the row alone. The holder's names its own receiver fields print (A3-G) are still read when the parser read the holder's name, and are switched off only by an account name that names the holder with a legal form or three words or more (a bare two-word fragment needs that corroboration): a bank that prints the owner as the receiver keeps the owner's own money held. Audit round 2, R2-B04 (R41, §20): the row-alone test also pairs American Express's unlabelled receiver block (`ONLINE TRANSFER PAYMENT CREDIT <originator> <entry> <id> <receiver> EXTERNAL …`, a `TRANSFER` entry included, on any statement's bank) with the originator, and with the payer region less its last word, the entry the template prints into it (`CONTOSO LLC PAYMENT`); and a row it reads that nothing claimed is the holder's own money even where an earlier rule verified it (step 11's accounts-payable entries, `DES:VENDOR PMT`, `DES:AP PAYMENT`, `DES:ACCOUNTS PAYABLE`, `DES:INVOICE PMT`, Chase's `CO ENTRY DESCR:VENDOR PAY`, whose holder guard reads only a known holder's name): pass 7 overrules the verification (method `self_originated_holder`, rule `holder_own_push_never_verified`, its reason naming the overruled method) and holds it as `holder_name`, and no own push, verified or not, counts toward a repeat. A credit under $1 keeps the floor's hold; a processor's settlement under the merchant's own name and a platform's payout id stay exempt. Audit round 2, R2-B04 (R04 parts 1 and 3, §20): nor does the holder test hold a processor's, a platform's or a franchisor's credit printing the merchant's own name: read outside the holder's names, its originator (the payer region, or the row's hold text when it prints none) prints a processor's or a platform's name (the operating registry's processors and platforms, their sixteen-character cuts, the bare platform names such as `INTUIT`, the card-payout processors), or ends with one of their one-word names cut by the bank's field (seven letters or more kept, at most two dropped, never a generic or holder-noise word: `MISC PAYMENT <holder> LIGHTSPEE`, while `PAYMENT` is no cut of `PAYMENTECH` nor `BUILDER` of `BUILDERTREND`), or the row prints a franchisor's or a platform's fee entry (`FRAN… FEE`, `ROYALTY FEE`, `MARKETING FEE`, `TECHNOLOGY FEES`, `ADVERTISING FEE`); never on an incoming wire or a person-to-person or push rail, which keep the hold; it lifts the hold only, never verifies, and the census's holder fact (G3) reads it too. Capital One's row is read without Mercury's tails, `MERCURYACH` after the originator and `MRCR` after the receiver, so the holder's own Mercury push is held whatever names the statement prints.
- **`payroll_or_salary`** (Q8), read on the row before its receiver fields:
  - `\bPAYROLL\w*`, `\bPAYRLL\b`, `\bPAYROL\b`, `\bPAYRL\b`, `\bSALARY\b`, `\bSALARIES\b`, `\bREG SAL\b`, `\bWAGES?\b`, `\bNET ?PAY\b`, `\bBONUS(?:ES)?\b`, `\bPAY PAY\b` (BMO's pay code), `\bPAIE\b`, `\bSALAIRES?\b`;
  - `\bDIR ?DEP\b`, `\bDIRDEP\b` and `\bDIRECT DEP\b` (not followed by `OSIT`) anywhere in the row, so Wells Fargo's `FABRIKAM INC DIR DEP 240115 …` is held; the whole phrase `DIRECT DEPOSIT` only as an entry description (`\b(?:DES|CO ENTRY DESCR) DIRECT DEPOSIT\b`), because BMO and ATB print it as their channel template (`^DIRECT DEPOSIT`). Audit fix batch B7 (I09, §20; amends review I4, §18.2 item 6; Q8's own words are "Payroll" and "Salary"): a direct deposit is a channel, not a purpose, so these words are pay wording only with a sign of a person's pay: a payroll provider named on the row (the classifier's `ADP`, `PAYCHEX`, `GUSTO`, `CERIDIAN`), a person in the receiver field (`INDN`, `IND NAME`, `RECEIVER NAME`) or after the deposit's references (Wells Fargo's `… DIR DEP <date> <id> JOHN SMITH`; B7 review round 1, §20: a reference, or a trace label `TRN`, `TRACE`, `REF` or `ID` and its number, printed after the person is skipped; never the holder's own name), a consumer (`PPD` or `WEB`) entry, or an account that does not read as a business's (its account name prints no business product word, legal form or company form, and the statement shows no payroll debit of the holder's own). A company's direct deposit to a business with none of these (`<CARRIER> DIR DEP <date> <id>`, a carrier's weekly settlement to its delivery contractor) takes its payer's reading: Q6 on an ACH layout, `named_payer_unlabelled` on one that prints no ACH word (I37's owner question). Q7's refusal still reads the words alone;
  - an employee's reimbursement from a company: `\bEXP(?:ENSE)? REIMB\w*`;
  - audit fix batch B6 (I49, §20): an earned-wage access provider's push (the registry's `EARNED_WAGE_ACCESS_PAYERS`: `PAYACTIV`, `DAILYPAY`), a worker's wages from another company paid early;
  - or `_is_payroll_provider_row`.
- **`personal_income`:** `\b(?:XX)?SOC SEC\b`, `\bSSA\b`, `\bSSI\b`, `\bSUPP SEC\b`, `\b(?:XX)?VA BEN\w*`, `\bUNEMPLOY\w*`, `\bUI BEN\w*`, `\bCHI?LD ?SUPP\w*\b`, `\bCHILD ?CTC\b`, `\bPENSION\b`, `\bANNUIT\w*`, `\bRETIRE\w*`. Audit fix batch B8 (I28, §20): and a health savings account's distribution (`HSA ?DISTRIB…`, glued or spaced) and a life insurer's annuity payment its sixteen-character originator field cut (`<insurer> LIFE AN DISB…`).
- **`non_sale_purpose`** (new). Any of these, read on the row before its receiver fields after the posting labels are removed:
  - owner money, investment and intercompany: `\bINVEST\w*`, `\bDISTRIB(?!UTORS?\b|UTING\b)\w*` (a distributor paying is a customer), `\bDIVIDENDS?\b`, `\bEQUITY\b`, `\bCONTRIB\w*`, `\bSHAREHOLDER\w*`, `\bOWNERS?\b` (not `OWNER OP`, `OWNER OPERATOR`: a carrier paying an owner-operator), `\bSTOCK\b`, `\bINTERCO\w*`, `\bINTER COMPANY\b`, `\bDUE FROM\b`, `\bSWEEP\b`, `\bZBA\b`, `\bZERO BAL\w*`;
  - financing that carries no root: `\bREPAY\w*`, `\bPRINCIPAL\b`, `\bPAYOFF\b`, `\bPROMISSORY\b`, `\bCONVERTIBLE\b`, `\bTRANCHE\b`, `\bDRAWS?\b`, `\bDRAWDOWNS?\b`, `\bDISBURS\w*`;
  - deposits held for others: `\bESCROW\b`, `\bEARNEST\b`, `\bSECURITY DEPOSIT\b`, `\bDEPOSIT BACK\b`, `\bSURPLUS\b`;
  - gifts, grants and courts: `\bGRANTS?\b`, `\bDONATION\w*`, `\bGIFT\b` (not `GIFT CARD`), `\bCOURT\b`, `\bJUDGMENT\b`;
  - rebates, claims, reimbursements and interest: `\bREBATES?\b`, `\bCLAIMS?\b` (not on an `HCCLAIMPMT` entry or a healthcare payer's `CLAIM PMT`), `\bREIMB\w*`, `\bINTEREST\b`, `\bREWARDS?\b`, `\bCASH ?BACK\b`. A bank's own interest, dividend or reward credit prints no payer; it is held here rather than named `non_revenue` (M12 of the review: about $0.12M of interest and $0.31M of dividends in the memo's rows);
  - a public payer's refund or benefit (§7.6): the payer region matches a §7.6 payer pattern or the payer-name veto, and the row prints a §7.6 whole-row veto word (tax, refunds including a glued `MDREFUND`, `EIC`, benefits, pay, relief, grants, loans, family and court, claims and awards, assistance);
  - a healthcare payer's member or premium credit: a healthcare payer with `\bREIMB\w*`, `\bMEMBER\b`, `\bMBR\b`, `\bSUBSCRIBER\b` or `\bPREMIUM\b` (§7.7);
  - audit fix batch B8 (I28, §20; the MCA principle of 2026-09-30, standard G3): a gambling or prediction-market payout, held for review (the owner may make it `non_revenue`): the sportsbooks', casinos' and prediction markets' names (`DRAFT ?KINGS`, `CROWN <state> GAMING`, `FAN ?DUEL`, `BET ?MGM`, `CAESARS SPORTSBOOK`, Fanatics' betting forms, `ESPN ?BET`, `BET ?365`, `BET ?RIVERS`, `PRIZE ?PICKS`, `UNDERDOG FANTASY|SPORTS`, `KALSHI`, `POLYMARKET`, `CHUMBA`, `PULSZ`), `JACKPOT`, `WINNINGS` and `LOTTERY PRIZE`, unless the row is the payer's payment of a vendor (§7.5's accounts-payable wording, `VENDOR`, `INVOICE`, `PAYABLES`, `INV`) or a lottery's settlement with its retailer (`LOTTERY` beside `SETTLEMENT`, `SWEEP`, `COMMISSION` or `RETAILER`, standard G4). A bare `FANATICS`, `UNDERDOG` or `CAESARS` is a retailer's, any business's or the casino company's name;
  - audit fix batch B8 (I44, §20; a legal windfall is no operating earning): a lawsuit's, a class action's or a claims administrator's settlement (`LAWSUIT`, `LITIGAT…`, `CLASS ACTION`, `DATA BREACH`, `SETTLEMENT ADMIN…`, and the administrators Epiq, JND Legal, `KCC` before `LLC`, `CLASS` or `SETTLEMENT`, Angeion, Simpluris, Rust Consulting, Kroll Settlement, A B Data, Verita), and a lawyer's or client trust account's payer (`IOLTA`, `ESQ`, `LAW`, `ATTORNEY` on the payer region) paying `SETTLEMENT PROCEEDS` or `FUNDS`, unless the row is a payment of a vendor's invoice; a bare `SETTLEMENT` stays counted, never verified (§8.6). A settlement or closing agent (`<NAME> SETTLEMENT SERVICES`, `<NAME> CLOSING SERVICES`) is a title firm (A3, §20), its fee payments counted as a title firm's are;
  - audit fix batch B8 (routed by the B7 review round 2, §20): `DISB`, a disbursement's abbreviation, on an owner-shaped payer (`HOLDINGS`, `PARTNERS`, `PARTNERSHIP`, `LP`, `LLP`, `LLLP`, a venture, a family office, a trust or an estate), however often it repeats; an ordinary payer's `CASH DISB` is its payment to a vendor and keeps its reading.

  Audit fix batch B7 (I18, §20; the words read only as a purpose, as "a distributor paying is a customer" intends, with §7.11's street guard as the precedent): a word followed by a street suffix (`AVE`, `ST`, `RD`, `BLVD`, `DR`, `LN`, `WAY`, `CT`, `PL`, `HWY`, `PKWY`) is a street's name (`<n> GRANT AVE`); `OWNER` is not read in the ordering platform's `OWNER COM`, nor `OWNER` or `OWNERS` in an owners' or homeowners' association's name (`OWNERS ASSOC…`, `OWNERS ASSN`, cut to `OWNERS A`); `DISTRIB…` and `STOCK` are not read inside the payer's name field (an ACH originator, a wire's sender, a transfer's sender after `FROM`), inside a name that a company form ends (after a name word: B7 review round 1, §20, never after a word with a digit or a purpose, period, member's, owner's, memo, field-label, connector or company-form word, so `QUARTERLY DISTRIBUTION <NAME> LLC` and `MEMO DISTRIBUTION <NAME> LLC` still hold; B7 review round 2, §20: a word with a digit is a name word when it holds a letter, at most two digits and no period's code, so `A1 DISTRIBUTION LLC` is a name and `Q4`, `K1`, `FY…`, `2054Q4`, `4TH` and a reference such as `AB1234` are not, and a tax, year-end, month, partnership, half-year, preferred, estimated or retained word is a purpose: `TAX DISTRIBUTION <NAME> LLC`, `MEMO YEAR END DISTRIBUTION <NAME> LLC` still hold), or in a joint-stock company's or bank's form (`JOINT STOCK COMPANY`, `JOINT STOCK COMMERCIAL BANK`), and `STOCK` not on a row that cites an invoice (`INV <n> STOCK AND FREIGHT`); `DISBURS…` is not read in the payer's own `DISBURSEMENT ACCOUNT`, nor as the disbursement entry of an ACH payer that repeats in the statement (two or more such credits) and reads as none of a lender, an insurer or claim payer, a bank, a title, escrow, closing or settlement firm, a lawyer's account, a public payer or (B7 review round 1, §20) an owner-shaped payer (a holding company, a partnership: `PARTNERS`, `PARTNERSHIP`, `LP`, `LLP`, `LLLP`; a venture, a family office, a trust or an estate, never `REAL ESTATE`) (`PSP <phone> DISBURSMNT`, a processor's payouts), unless `LOAN`, `ESCROW`, `TITLE` or `PROCEEDS` stands beside it. A purpose, an entry field or a row that prints no name field still holds them (`DES:DISTRIB`, `QUARTERLY PARTNER DISTRIBUTION`, `FOR STOCK PURCHASE`, a one-off `DISBURSEMENT`); a builder's progress `DRAW` waits for the owner's ruling (§15). Audit fix batch B7 (I47, §20): this hold, `possible_return`, `payroll_or_salary` and `personal_income` read the row without the holder's own name runs (the financing roots' removal, with the run's prefixes of two words or more for a holder the account name names).

  Measured on the memo's rows: $1.3M, 855 rows (of which the public-payer part is $0.16M, 407 rows).
- **`payer_also_debited`:** the payer's first distinctive word (§7.5's definition) is also the first distinctive word of a posted debit in the same statement that is not a lender debit. The payer may be the owner's other account, an affiliate, or a funder whose daily debits no rule names; the accounts-payable verifier's R2 required review. It covers the §7.5 counter-debit rows (`payer_remittance_with_counter_debit`, 19 rows, $242K) and the Q5 and Q6 counter-debit guards (§8.5, §8.6). A payer debited as a lender is `payer_debited_as_lender` instead. Audit fix batch B4 (§20: I02, I38, I56) narrows it to the debits that can be circular money, as its purpose states: the bank's own receiving fee for an incoming wire or funds transfer never holds the payer; a processor's own fee, discount, chargeback, adjustment or returns debit never holds a credit that prints settlement, payout, `MID` or merchant-services wording; a bill the holder pays (bill pay, EBPP, autopay, a utility, telecom, cable or subscription) never holds a payer that does not read as an insurer; except that a fee or bill debit for the credit's own amount, or a bill debit that is one of two or more at one amount, still holds (a processor's fixed fee recurs at one amount by nature). The debit's kind is read without its receiver fields' values, the holder's own name. A transfer, wire, person-to-person or own-account debit matches at any size. Generic words and banks' channel names never make the first word (`MERCHANT`, `EXPRESS`, `UTILITIES` …), a name glued on one side matches it spaced on the other, and a three-letter payroll provider (`ADP`) is a word. Audit round 2, R2-B02 (§20; standard A1, A2, A6, §4.2), in this order: (R18) a platform's payout and the platform's own pull, each printing a payout id (`holder_name` above: the merchant's descriptor on both, Stripe's refund and negative-balance pulls), never hold unless the pull is for the payout's own amount; then the holds above in their order; then (R01) a processor's or a platform's payout, read by its settlement wording or a payout form before the receiver fields (`MRCHNTSVCS`, `MRCH SVCS`, `COMB DEP`, Payment Brands' `PAYMNTBRAND` and `PYMNTBRDMIN`, `INTUIT CANADA PAYMENTS` and its cut `INTUIT CANADA P`, `DEPOSIT <bank> MERCHANT`, a twelve-character payout id of letters and digits after `ST`) or by a merchant-ID field anywhere on the row (`MID <six digits or more>`, which Chase and Truist print after the receiver), is never held by the processor's card charge or by a one-off debit of its own that reads no other kind (a subscription, a `CARD PMT`, a debit that prints only the processor's name and digits): only a circular or lender debit, a debit for the payout's own amount, or a non-card debit recurring at one amount still holds it. A card purchase printed in a form the card-form prefix misses (`DEBIT CARD PURCHASE`, `DBCRD PUR`, `POS DB`, `POS DEBIT`, `POS PURCHASE`, `POINT OF SALE`, a glued `PURCHASE<digits>`, a charge ending `<state or province> USA` or `CAN`, or a debit the classifier classes `pos`) is a card charge where it reads a fee, a bill or another debit, never a circular debit or the receiving fee; it keeps that kind and still counts for recurrence (R2-B02 review round 1: the card form only lifts a payout's hold; beside a credit with no payout form it holds as its kind does, so a bill or subscription printed in a card form reads the bill rule above). (R02) An ATM, ABM or cash withdrawal that is no circular money never holds a payer (it names a place, never a payer; review round 1: a company whose name leads with `ATM` or `ABM`, a legal form within its next two words, or an ACH entry printing one, is a payee, never a withdrawal), and the readings and the whole-name match of `payer_debited_as_lender` above apply. (R48 part 1) The bank's receiving fee reads its outgoing words on its label and the words before the sender it reprints after `TT` or `FROM`, never inside that sender's name, so `FUNDS TRANSFER FEE TT CONTOSO TO G` is the fee for a wire from `CONTOSO TO GO INC`, never an outgoing-wire fee; a bare `WIRE TRANSFER FEE <name>` reads as before. Review round 1: `FROM` before the holder's own account (an account word or a masked account number) or before `TO` starts no sender, so `WIRE TRANSFER FEE FROM CHECKING TO <payee>` is the fee for the holder's own wire; `OUTGOING`, `BNF` and `BENEFICIARY`, which no sender's name prints, are read after the sender too.
- **`non_sale_purpose` after the counter-debit holds** (audit fix batch B4, §20: I56): a payroll provider's tax-service credit (`ADP`, `PAYCHEX`, `GUSTO` or `INTUIT` with `TAX` or `TAXES`, the tax word read on the payer region and the entry fields, never on the holder's name a label-less layout prints in the row) that no debit holds is the holder's own tax impound coming back.

### 8.2 Q2: cash, branch, ATM and bare deposits; transfer wording

The patterns follow the memo's `categorize.py`, which agrees with 94% of 315 hand-sorted payer groups; every pattern that file holds is inlined below. The cash, branch and ATM patterns apply only to a row that prints no electronic-payment form and no person-to-person rail, so an ACH payer named like a branch (`BRANCH MESSENGER`) is never read as a teller deposit.
- **Cash:** `\bCASH DEP(?:OSIT)?\b`, `\bATM CASH\b`, `\bCPS DEVICE\b`, `\bCURRENCY PROCESSING\b`, `\bSMART ?SAFE\b`, `\bCOIN\b`, `\bCASH IN\b`.
- **Branch and teller:** `\bCOUNTER (?:CREDIT|DEPOSIT)\b`, `\bTELLER\b`, `\bIN BRANCH\b`, `\bBRANCH\b`, `\bBRCH\b`, `\bDEPOSIT AT BR\b`, `\bDEPOSIT THANK YOU\b`, `\bMAIN OFFICE\b`, `\bPRE ?ENCODED\b`, `\bNIGHT (?:DEP|DROP)\b`, `\bBANKING CENTRE\b`, `^GC\b.*\bDEPOSIT\b`, `\bG DEPOT\b`, `\bDEPOT\b`, `\bDEPOSIT IBB\b`, `\bVAULT DEP\w*`, `\bSTORE DEPOSIT\b`, `\bDEPOSIT MADE IN\b`.
- **ATM:** `\bATM\b.*\bDEP(?:OSIT)?\b`, `\bDEP(?:OSIT)?\b.*\bATM\b`, `\bABM\b`.
- These three are `counted_flagged` / `no_payer_printed`, never Verified (§6.1 step 9b).
- **Bare deposit, credit or EFT,** on any channel: after the template prefixes, `GENERIC_WORDS` and the reference tokens are removed, no payer word remains, and the row prints no transfer wording. It is `counted_flagged` / `no_payer_printed` too (§8.10, last step), never Verified. BMO's `DIRECT DEPOSIT <digits>` is a numbered company (§5.2) instead.
- **A payer the parser did not read** (audit fix batch B10: I05, I60, §20): Q2 covers a row whose statement prints no payer, never a row whose parser lost the payer the statement printed. A deterministic parser marks a credit it publishes with only its own posting-type or section label because it could not read the printed payer (`Transaction.payer_text_unread`: a core-processor merchant block the attribution count cannot give to one row, the daily core-processor template's every credit block while its reading waits on G7 (a teller's reference aside, which names no payer; audit fix batch B10 review round 1), First Citizens' `Other Credit` where its description overlay fails closed); such a row, where the pass would read it as a bare deposit (§8.10's last step), is `counted_flagged` / `payer_text_not_read` instead of `no_payer_printed`, never Verified. Every hold before it still applies (a marked bare transfer label stays `transfer_no_counterparty`), and a cash, branch, teller or ATM label keeps `no_payer_printed` marked or not, since the label itself says the statement printed no payer (B10 review round 1). Whether such rows should be held instead would change this section's rule and is the owner's; the statement carries the fact `payer_text_unread` when more than 30% of its posted credit rows are marked (§9.1). Audit round 2, R2-B07 (R07, §20): the counted core-processor template keeps, on each credit it marks at a welded flush, that flush's joined text (`Transaction.unread_payer_text`, §9.1), the blocks of every row outstanding at it; a marked row that would be `payer_text_not_read` is held as `unread_run_names_financing` instead when one block of that text (split after each ACH class code) names a registry lender of a lender or financing state other than an alternative position or a factor (§8.1, Q6: lenders never count) or a lending bank (`LENDING_BANK_CREDIT_PAYERS`, T4). The bare financing root is not read (a customer named `CAPITAL` stays `payer_text_not_read`), the text never verifies, never names the row's payer and never puts an amount toward lender credits, and the review card prints it so a reviewer can release a customer's row the lender's block held. Which row of the run is the lender's stays unknown: pairing blocks by printed order is not done (B10 measured out-of-order flushes).
- **Transfer wording:** `\bTRANSFER\b`, `\bTRNSFR\b`, `\bXFER\b`, `\bTFR\b`, `\bBR TO BR\b`, `\bONLINE BANKING\b`, `\bINTERNET (?:BANKING|TRANSFER)\b`, `\bFUNDS TRANSFER\b`, on a row that prints no person-to-person rail. Audit fix batch B8 (I46, §20): and the plural `\bTRANSFERS\b` (`EXTERNAL DEPOSIT ACH TRANSFERS PAYMENT`, `… FUNDS TRANSFERS`), now that §7.0's revenue text cuts TD's back panel, which prints `… ELECTRONIC FUNDS TRANSFERS`; the holds' posting labels drop `FUNDS TRANSFERS` as they drop `FUNDS TRANSFER`. Audit round 2, R2-B09 (§20): American Express's label `ONLINE TRANSFER PAYMENT CREDIT` at the row's start is no transfer wording only when an originator word follows it (R31: the bare label, or the label before `FROM`, an account word, `ID`, a masked account or digits, is a bare transfer, held); the glued `ONLINETRANSFER`, `MOBILETRANSFER` and `INTERNETTRANSFER` (singular or plural) are transfer wording, the glued label at the row's start under the same originator rule (R45); and on a credit, transfer wording printed inside a company-formed ACH originator's own name (before `DES`, after `ORIG CO NAME`: `CONTOSO TRF LLC`, `TRF INDUSTRIES INC`, `CONTOSO TRANSFER INC`) is the company's name, never a transfer, so the row is an ACH payer Q6 reads (R28); the entry field's wording (`DES:TRANSFER`, `CO ENTRY DESCR:BANK XFER`) is still read, and a name with no company form once the wording is out (`<NAME> BANK XFER`, `TRANSFER AND STORAGE CO`) keeps the transfer reading. A debit's reading and Q6's entry-field refusal are unchanged. R2-B09 review round 1 (§20): `DES` opens that name only with a field of Bank of America's layout after its entry (`ID`, `INDN`, `CO ID`, `IND NAME`, `IND ID` or an ACH class code), never as a town's word (`TRANSFER FROM <NAME> LLC DES MOINES IA`), and a name printing a transfer's phrase word (`FROM`, `FRM`, `FR`, `TO`, `BETWEEN`) is no company's own name: such a named transfer keeps its transfer reading.
  - With no counterparty (no payer word after the template prefixes, `GENERIC_WORDS`, the account words of T13 and the reference tokens are removed), it is `excluded_pending_review` / `transfer_no_counterparty`. The tier pass tests this **before** the check, cash, branch and ATM tiers, so `TELLER TRANSFER` is held, not counted.
  - With a named counterparty, T13 and §8.4 apply.
- **A pull that names only a bank** (audit round 2, R2-B03: R05, §20; a bank's name is never the payer's name): an ACH or unlabelled credit whose payer region is only a listed sending bank's name (the registry's `P2P_SENDING_BANK_NAMES`, which now lists `MSPBNA` and `ALLY BANK`) or one to four words ending `BANK`, `BANCORP`, `FSB` or `CREDIT UNION` that read as a bank by its own form (never a food or blood bank, never a company whose name holds the bank word, never a bank's settlement form), a trailing `ACH`, `ONLINE` or `N A` aside, printed with no entry field, is the holder's pull from another of its own bank accounts (`ACH CREDIT <bank>` on Relay, `<bank> ACH` on Brex, `ELECTRONIC DEPOSIT FROM <bank>` on U.S. Bank): `excluded_pending_review` / `transfer_no_counterparty`, unless a rule verified it or the registry, a payout entity, a factoring payee or a factor claimed it. The statement's own bank's channel (`CHASE ONLINE` on a Chase statement) is never held: a customer's bill payment through the bank, counted with no payer (§8.6).

### 8.3 Q3: check deposits

`\bMOBILE (?:CHECK |CHEQUE )?(?:DEPOSIT|DEP)\b`, `\bMOBILE CHE(?:CK|QUE)\b`, `\bMB DEP\b`, `\bBKOFAMERICA MOBILE\b`, `\bREMOTE (?:ONLINE )?(?:DEPOSIT|CAPTURE)\b`, `\bDEPOSIT CAPTURE\b`, `\bMERCHANT CAPTURE\b`, `\bDESKTOP (?:REMOTE )?DEPOSIT\b`, `\bEDEPOSIT SCAN\b`, `\bE ?CHEQUE DEPOSIT\b`, `\b(?:ATM )?CHE(?:CK|QUE)S? (?:DEPOSIT|DEP|RECEIVED)\b`, `\bMAIL DEPOSIT\b`, `^REMOTE$`, `\bIMAGED DEPOSIT\b`, `\bDEPOSITED (?:CHECK|ITEM)\b`, `\bRDC\b`, `\bSCAN(?:NED)? DEPOSIT\b`, `\bLOCKBOX\b`, `\bLBX DEP\b`.

These are `counted_flagged` / `check_deposit`, never Verified (§6.1 step 9b). A returned deposited item is decided earlier, as a reversal.

### 8.4 Q4: person-to-person, and named transfers

- **Rails:** the consumer rails `\bZELLE\b`, `\bINTERAC\b`, `\bE ?TRANSFER\b`, `\bE ?TFR\b`, `\bVIREMENT\b`, `\bVFC\b`, `\bAUTODEPOSIT\b`, `\bVENMO\b`, `\bCASH ?APP\b`, `\bSQUARE CASH\b`, `\bAPPLE CASH\b`, `\bPOPMONEY\b`, and the real-time and push rails `\bREAL TIME (?:TRANSFER|PAYMENT)\b`, `\bRTP\b`, `\bFEDNOW\b`, `\bINSTANT (?:PMT|PAYMENT|TRANSFER)\b`, `\bVISA (?:MONEY TRANSFER|DIRECT)\b`, `\bMONEY TRANSFER\b`, `\bMC SEND\b`, `\bPMNT RCVD\b`, `\bPAYMENT RECEIVED\b`. A row the registry or a form already claimed (an RTP payout from Amazon, a Visa Direct payout from Square) is not a person-to-person row.
- **Named transfers** (T13): transfer wording (§8.2) with a named counterparty belongs here.
- **The sender** is the payer region (§5.2).
- **`transfer_account_designation`:** a named transfer whose row prints an account designation, an account-type word (`CHECKING`, `CHK`, `CHKG`, `SAVINGS`, `SAVING`, `SAV`, `SVGS`, `MONEY MARKET`, `MMA`, `DDA`, `ACCOUNT`, `ACCT`, `EVERYDAY`; audit round 2, R2-B09: R45, §20: and `CHCKING`, `CHKING`, `CKG`, which name no counterparty either) or a masked account number (`\bX{2,}\d{2,}\b`, `\bENDING (?:IN )?\d{3,}\b`), is held: it reads as the owner's account at another bank. Measured on the memo's rows: $1.18M, 1,473 rows, 168 statements.
- **`p2p_round_repeat`:** a sender with two or more round-sum credits on these rails or through named transfers in the statement has every one of its round-sum credits held. Its other credits stay counted. The memo counted a repeat of any amount; this reading follows the ruling's words, "repeated round sums from one sender".
- A sender with the holder's name is `holder_name` (§8.1).
- Everything else is `counted_flagged` / `p2p`, never Verified, even when the sender prints a company form (Q4, §6.1 step 9b).

### 8.5 Q5: incoming wires

- **The wire family:** the leading labels of §7.9 N1, a third-party book transfer (§7.11, only with T19), or, for tiering only, the loose wire words `\bWIRE\b`, `\bFEDWIRE\b`, `\bFED WIRE\b`, `\bIWT\b`, `\bCHIPS CREDIT\b`, `\bSWIFT\b`, `\bFOREIGN REMITTANCE\b`, `\bWT FED\b`, `\bFED REF\b` on a row no rail of §8.4 claimed. Audit fix batch B5 (I14, I12's reader, §20): Navy Federal's `BANK WIRE DEPOSIT`, Columbia Bank's `WIRE DEPOSIT WIRE IN` and U.S. Bank's `WIRE CREDIT REF<n>` are N1 labels, and a loose-wire row's originator label (`ORIG`, `ORG`, `B O`, `ORIGINATOR`; never `ORIG CO` or `ORIG ID`) names its sender as an incoming wire's does. Audit round 2, R2-B10 (R26, §20): only a label printed after the row's first `WIRE` word, read from its own position; a label word before it is a word of the sender's own name on the `<sender> WIRE` layout (Mercury, Brex: `CONTOSO ORG LLC WIRE`, a `.org` domain), so the whole name is the payer region.
- **Verified: the same company twice or more** (method `repeat_wire_sender`, confidence 0.85). The row begins with an N1 label (or is a third-party book transfer under T19), and:
  - no hold of §8.1 applies to it;
  - its sender prints a company form (§5.2) or is a government or healthcare payer; audit fix batch B5 (I61, §20): here only, an `LLC` the 35-character originator field cut to `LL` and glued to the address after it (`<name> MANAGEMENT LL<city>`) is a company form; never a two-letter cut of another form (`CO`, `IN`, `LI`), and never for Q6;
  - two or more wire credits in the statement have that payer key (repeats, §5.2, which never count a held row);
  - the sender is not a numbered company (§5.2), and names no bank (`BANK`, `BANCO`, `BANCORP`, `N A`, `TRUST COMPANY`, `CREDIT UNION`, `FCU`, `SAVINGS`), since a relaying bank hides the real payer, and no title or escrow firm (`TITLE`, `ESCROW`, `CLOSING`, `SETTLEMENT`);
