- **The counter-debit hold reads the debit's kind** (§8.1 `payer_also_debited`, T3; audit fix batch B4: I02; standard A1, A2, A5, §4.2; amends §8.1's "a posted debit … that is not a lender debit", which the owner approved in bulk at §19, to the hold's own stated purpose: an own account, an affiliate or a funder that collects): a non-lender, non-card debit holds the payer only when it can be circular money. (a) The bank's own receiving fee for an incoming wire or funds transfer, which reprints the sender (RBC's `FUNDS TRANSFER FEE TT <sender>`, `WIRE TRANSFER FEE [FROM] <sender>`, `[DOMESTIC|INTERNATIONAL] INCOMING WIRE FEE`, `WIRE TRANS SVC CHARGE`, `WIRE FEE`), never holds it; a wire fee printing `OUTGOING`, `OUT`, `TO`, `BNF` or `BENEFICIARY` (the fee for the holder's own wire to the payer) still does. (b) A processor's own fee, discount, chargeback, adjustment or returns debit (`FEE[S]`, `DISCOUNT`, `DISC`, `CHBK`, `CHGBK`, `CHRGBACK`, `CHARGEBACK[S]`, `ADJ`, `ADJUSTMENT[S]`, `RETURN[S]`) never holds a credit that prints settlement, payout, `MID` or `MERCHANT SERVI…` wording. (c) A bill the holder pays (`BILL PAY[MENT]`, `BILLPAY`, `BILL PAID`, `BILL PMT`, `EBPP`, `AUTOPAY…`, `AUTO PAY`, `UTIL…`, `TELECOM`, `WIRELESS`, `CABLE`, `VOIP`, `SUBSCRIPTION`) never holds a payer that does not read as an insurer (`INS`, `INSUR…`, `ASSUR…`): an insurer's credit beside its premium stays held (Q6). A fee or bill debit for the credit's own amount, or one of two or more debits of the payer's word at one amount ("recurring same-amount debits keep holding"), still holds; no materiality floor is added. A transfer, wire, person-to-person or own-account debit (`TO CHKG`, `DDA TO DDA`, `ZEL`) holds at any size. Registered processors stay exempt as claimed. Audit I02's unconfirmed processor settlements (their debits print only digits, so they cannot be shown to be fees) and an insurer's premium-coded credits beside its premium debits stay held (amended by audit round 2, R2-B02: R01, below: those settlements print their processor's merchant-ID field after the receiver, and Payment Brands, a card payment facilitator, debits the merchant for its fees, refunds and chargebacks only, so they are counted; the insurer's premium-coded credits stay held). 7d2b31d.
- **Generic words and banks' channel names never make the counter-debit word** (§8.1, §7.5's first distinctive word; audit fix batch B4: I02): pass 6 and pass 7 read the payer and the debit without `MERCHANT`, `UTILITIES`, `EXPRESS`, `INCOME`, `PAIEMENT`, `POSTED`, `SERVICE`, `SERVICES`, `TRSFR` and the bank names the corpus prints as a channel (`CHASE`'s online payment and card, `TRUIST`'s merchant deposit and ATM, `BLUEVINE`'s pay product and plan fee), so `<initials> EXPRESS INC` never matches American Express and `MERCHANT SERVICES` never matches another processor's fee. A bank name printed as the holder's own account at another bank (`BKOFAMERICA … FR CHKG`, `WELLS FARGO … DDA TO DDA`, `JPMORGAN CHASE ACH`, a Mercury transfer) is not listed, and the guard keeps the 5.6 reading: a transfer, wire, person-to-person, own-account or lender debit still matches on 5.6's first word, and so does any debit for the credit's own amount. 7d2b31d.
- **Pass 6 reads the same debit kinds** (§7.5, §6.2 pass 6; audit fix batch B4: I02): the accounts-payable counter-debit guard calls pass 7's reader (`counter_debits`, `debited_as_lender`, `also_debited`), so a bill, a processor's fee or the bank's receiving fee never demotes a remittance (`CONTOSO UTILITIES DES:VENDOR PAY` beside `BILL PAY CONTOSO UTILITIES` stays verified); a lender debit is compared by whole words and registry entity, never by prefix (pass 6 demotes a verified row). The corpus moved 0 rows through pass 6. 7d2b31d.
- **A funder's cut, glued or referenced name** (§8.1 `payer_debited_as_lender`; audit fix batch B4: I22): a token's glued digit run is split off before the words are read (`<NAME>USA1` reads `<NAME>USA`; never a masked run of X); a payer and a lender debit that both name a registry lender or financing entity match when it is one entity (two entities never match except by 5.6's first word); otherwise, for an unverified credit in pass 7, one first word of five letters or more that starts the other matches when the longer runs on into a financing root (`FUND`, `FIN`, `CAP`, `LEND`, `LOAN`, `ADV`, `CRED`, `MCA`) or into the shorter side's next word (`<NAME> VENTURE` against `<NAME>VENTUREHO`), never into anything else (a customer named like a funder). The guard is narrower than the audit's plain prefix; on the corpus the two hold the same rows. Two funders whose debits print an abbreviation or a three-letter name resolve when their aliases land (audit batch B6). Measured: 2 wires (about $14K) from `wire_named_sender` to `payer_debited_as_lender`, and 7 Forward Financing fundings printed `FORWARDFINUSA<ref>` beside Forward's own debits, $174,950.33, from `named_payer_one_off` to `payer_debited_as_lender` (audit I08's rows, which B6 names in the registry). 7d2b31d.
- **A name glued on one side and spaced on the other** (§8.1 both counter-debit holds; audit fix batch B4: I38): the payer's and the debit's first distinctive word are compared with their next one or two words glued on (`<NAME> RIDGE` matches `<NAME>RIDGE`). Measured: one counted credit of over $1M, a lump sum whose payer the holder's own debit prints glued, is held as `payer_also_debited` (its nature stays unconfirmed, audit I38; the three other months print no glued debit and stay counted), and one Q6 company-form credit (four figures) beside the holder's ACH purchase from the same company printed glued, is held (a declared demotion, `q5_q6_held_counter_debit`). 7d2b31d.
- **A payroll provider's tax-service credit** (§8.1, §8.10 after item 11; audit fix batch B4: I56; standard E1, G1): a three-letter payroll provider (`ADP`) is a counter-debit word, so an `ADP TAX` credit beside the holder's own `ADP TAX` debits is held as `payer_also_debited`; a payroll provider's tax-service credit (`ADP`, `PAYCHEX`, `GUSTO` or `INTUIT` with `TAX` or `TAXES` before the receiver fields) that no debit holds is held as `non_sale_purpose` (the holder's own tax impound coming back), on a claimed row too (the pay holds' convention); the census's `hold_rule` reads it as `payroll_tax`. A tax preparer's customer (`<NAME> TAX SERVICES LLC`) is no payroll provider. Measured: 12 ADP credits, $9,660.80, and 3 Paychex and Intuit tax credits, $318.37, leave True. The glued carbon rebate of the same audit item waits for batch B8. 7d2b31d.
- **The census for audit fix batch B4** (§14.2, §14.3): `LATER_HOLD_CAUSES` declares a Q5 or Q6 row that a counter-debit hold names with no other hold reading, `("payer_also_debited", None)` as `q5_q6_held_counter_debit` and `("payer_debited_as_lender", None)` as `q5_q6_held_lender_counter_debit`; a counter-debit hold beside another hold reading, or another rule's row, stays undeclared. 7d2b31d.
- **Audit fix batch B4 in all** (B3 review round 1's tree to this one, input basis): True 68.230% → 68.436% (+$569,253.61, +0.301% of True), Verified 28.012% → 28.365% (+$977,078.24: 83 rows promoted, 44 Q6 repeats beside a bill and 11 Q5 repeat wires beside the bank's receiving fee among them; 1 row demoted and declared), held 17.361% → 17.155%, non-revenue 14.409% unchanged; `payer_also_debited` $10,599,500.37 (2,828 rows) → $9,811,763.06 (2,566 rows): 276 rows, $1,951,090.18, leave it (270 of them, $1,922,190.18, into True; 6 person-to-person round sums stay held as `p2p_round_repeat`) and 14 rows, $1,163,352.87, newly enter it; statements with $0 True 135 → 131; the census sweep's class C (financing) 18 rows → 11 and class G (government money) 13 rows → 1; every checked guard holds and no row leaves `non_revenue`; no credit whose 5.6-matching debit is a transfer, wire, person-to-person or lender debit changed tier; 0 of 676,525 debits and 0 lender, flag or eligible-deposit readings moved (G7 with the owner's allowlist: 0 moves). 7d2b31d.
- **B4 review round 1: the debit's kind is read without the holder's name** (§8.1 `payer_also_debited`, §7.5 pass 6; amends the B4 line "The counter-debit hold reads the debit's kind" above): the kind reader reads a debit without its receiver fields' values (`INDN`, `IND NAME`, `RECEIVER NAME`, `RECV NAME`, `IND ID`: the holder's own name), as the lender-entity reader already did, every field after them kept (a `PMT INFO` addenda). A holder whose name carries a bill or fee word (`<NAME> CABLE LLC`, `<NAME> UTILITY SERVICES INC`, `<NAME> WIRELESS LLC`, `<NAME> RETURNS LLC`) no longer turns an affiliate's or an unregistered collector's ACH debit into a bill or a processor's fee, so that payer stays held as at the B3 tree; a kind word printed in the payer's own fields or in the addenda still reads. Measured: 0 rows move (the exposure was latent). e647dec.
- **B4 review round 1: a processor's fee at one amount never re-holds its settlements** (§8.1; standard §4.2; amends the same B4 line's "or one of two or more debits of the payer's word at one amount ('recurring same-amount debits keep holding'), still holds" for the processor-fee kind): a processor's fixed monthly or per-batch fee posts at one amount by nature (a two-month statement prints the monthly fee twice), and the fee exclusion already needs the credit to print settlement, payout, `MID` or merchant-services wording, which a funding does not print; the recurrence exception now keeps only a bill holding (a bill-pay or autopay debit at one amount, weekly, can be a collector's draw). A processor's fee for the credit's own amount still holds. Measured: 0 rows move. e647dec.
- **B4 review round 1: the payroll provider's tax word is read on the payer's own fields** (§8.1, §8.10 after item 11; amends the B4 I56 line's "with `TAX` or `TAXES` before the receiver fields"): the provider word is still read on the row before the receiver fields, the `TAX` or `TAXES` word only on the payer region and the entry fields, never on the rest of the row, where a layout with no receiver label prints the holder's name: a payroll provider paying a holder named for taxes (`<PROVIDER> DEPOSIT <ref> <NAME> TAX SERVICES LLC`) is counted as at the B3 tree, while a layout that prints its entry before the provider (`ACH TAXES <ref> PAYCHEX TPS`) reads it as the payer region and stays held. Measured: the old and new readings agree on all 3,846 cached credits that print a payroll provider word; 0 rows move. e647dec.
- **B4 review round 1: the funder prefix, like the first-word match, comes before Q5 and Q6** (§8.1 `payer_debited_as_lender`; states what the B4 line "A funder's cut, glued or referenced name" means by "for an unverified credit"): an unverified credit is one no rule verified, as the first-word match reads it, so a glued funder name (`<NAME>FUNDING`) holds a Q6 company-form or repeat payer (`<NAME> EVENTS LLC`) exactly as the spaced name (`<NAME> FUNDING`) already did at the B3 tree (I38: a name glued on one side matches it spaced on the other); the census declares such a row `q5_q6_held_lender_counter_debit`. Pinned by a test; no code change; no corpus row that Q5 or Q6 would verify is held this way. e647dec.
- **B4 review round 1: `ADP` is read as any payer** (§8.1, §7.5 pass 6; notes the B4 I56 line): with `ADP` a counter-debit word, an accounts-payable remittance from ADP beside the holder's payroll-fee debit to ADP is demoted (`payer_remittance_with_counter_debit`), as a Paychex, Gusto or any four-letter payer's already was at the B3 tree (the remittance prints no settlement wording, so the fee debit holds it). Kept: the brief makes `ADP` a word like its siblings, and an ADP credit beside the holder's ADP debits is most often ADP's own money coming back. Pinned by a test; no code change. e647dec.
- **B4 review round 1 in all** (7d2b31d's tree to this one, input basis): 0 of 901,548 walked rows move over 7,241 cached inputs (credits and debits, every revenue, lender, flag and `reversed_by` field); the census snapshot and the G7 lender-census snapshot are byte-identical to 7d2b31d's; True 68.436% and Verified 28.365% unchanged; the census compare holds every guard, with no row leaving `non_revenue` and no verified demotion; G7 with the owner's allowlist: 0 moves. e647dec.
- **A wire's sender is never a bank's or a layout's label** (§8.5 "Held", §5.2's wire payer region, §7.9 N1; audit fix batch B5: I14, I12's reader; Q5): on pass 7's wire channel a payer region with no name word (a lone glued reference, `<ref>S<ref>WIRE`) or made only of bank and layout labels (`BANK`, `DEPOSIT`, `INTERNATIONAL`, `IN`, `INCOMING`, `RECEIVED`, `DEP` and the bare channel words of the A3-G line) names no sender: `wire_no_sender`, held, where it read `wire_named_sender` (counted) on the loose-wire layouts. `IWT` is not such a label until audit batch B10 keeps the sender Falcon Bank prints after its `INTERNATIONAL IWT` label (those rows keep their reading). Navy Federal's `BANK WIRE DEPOSIT`, Columbia Bank's `WIRE DEPOSIT WIRE IN` and U.S. Bank's `WIRE CREDIT REF<n>` are N1 labels, and a loose-wire row's originator label (`ORIG`, `ORG`, `B O`, `ORIGINATOR`; never `ORIG CO`, `ORIG ID` or `ORG TO BNF`) names its sender as an incoming wire's does, so the holder test, the financing roots, the bank-originated hold and Q5's key read the sender after `ORG` (U.S. Bank's `ORG=<sender>`, once its parser keeps that line in audit batch B10). Owner decision I12 (whether ordinary-size wires with no printed sender count, flagged) is open and would apply to these rows too; whether the two layouts print a remitter on a line the extractor dropped stays unconfirmed. Measured: 49 Navy Federal rows (15 statements) and 36 TD Canada Trust glued-reference rows (3 statements) move from counted to held, $347,082.93; Columbia's third-party `ORG` senders stay counted. 346881a. Amended by audit round 2, R2-B10 (below): the label list is every wire layout word plus `CR` (R32), and a loose-wire label is read only after the row's first `WIRE` word (R26).
- **A bank's own wire is held on every wire row** (§8.1 `financing_named_payer`, the A3-G line; audit fix batch B5: I14, I23; the orchestrator's ruling that a wire a bank originates on its own behalf is held): the bank-originated reading runs on every row pass 7's wire channel reads (Mercury's `<sender> WIRE`, a loose wire with an originator label), not only on a row an N1 label starts; never on a region of labels alone (`BANK WIRE DEPOSIT` names no sender and is never financing). Whether the held credits are loan proceeds or the holder's own money stays unconfirmed. Measured: 2 rows (a bank-named five-figure wire and a UK bank's four-figure `ORG` wire) move from counted to held. 346881a.
- **Chase's real-time sender is read after its contra bank** (§5.2's person-to-person payer region; audit fix batch B5: I15): `RECD FROM ABA/CONTR BNK-<routing> FROM: <sender>` reads the sender after the second `FROM`, never the contra bank's label, so every region-based hold and the round-sum key read the real sender (two customers' round sums are two senders). Measured: 4 rows of a commission-advance company, $15,169.00, are held as `financing_named_payer` (whether its advances are financing or factoring-like is a §15 source, unruled), and 38 wallet instant-transfer rows (35 counted, $78,823.00; 3 already held as round sums) now read `VENMO` and are held as `payer_also_debited` beside the holder's own Venmo debits (audit B4's circular-money hold; the audit tied this part to I02's ruling, which B4 kept; amended by audit round 2, R2-B02: R02, below: a wallet's cash-out names no sender, so the counter-debit holds no longer read `VENMO` as its payer and these rows are counted as `p2p` again). 346881a.
- **PNC's `CORPORATE ACH` payer is read after its entry and reference** (§5.2's ACH payer region and entry fields; audit fix batch B5: I15): `CORPORATE ACH <entry> <reference> <payer> <reference>`, the entry one or two words of ten characters at most, reads the payer after the reference, and the entry is an entry field the holds and Q6 read (`FUNDING` holds, `SETTLEMENT` refuses); a row with no such entry and reference keeps its reading. Different payers no longer pool under the entry word. The card-settlement form `intuit_deposit` reads PNC's `CORPORATE ACH DEPOSIT <ref> INTUIT <ref>`, the QuickBooks Payments merchant deposit every other layout prints `INTUIT <ref> DEPOSIT` (the brief's landing check: once the payer read `INTUIT`, the holder's own QuickBooks card charge would have held these deposits as `payer_also_debited`). Measured: 30 Intuit rows, $193,046.23, counted → Verified (`operating_income_descriptor`, QuickBooks Payments); 10 rows, $31,111.00, counted → Verified as real repeats; 2 rows, $736.25, Verified → counted `named_payer_one_off` (declared `q5_q6_payer_after_a_label`). True unchanged. 346881a.
- **An ACH field label printed with no value names no payer** (§5.2, §8.2, §8.6; audit fix batch B5: I55; Q2): `ORIG CO` with no `NAME` and no value (a Chase export that keeps only the label: `ORIG CO ACH credit`) is no payer and `CO ENTRY DESCR` starts the entry field; the bare-row test reads `ORIG CO NAME`, `ORIG CO`, `CO ENTRY DESCR`, `ORIG ID`, `IND NAME` and `IND ID` as labels only (`CO` alone stays a name word). Such a row reads `no_payer_printed`, never Verified. Measured: 7 rows of one statement, $15,818.52, Verified → counted (declared `channel_gate`: the row now prints no payer word). 346881a.
- **A payment reference before the payer is no numbered company** (§5.2 numbered company, T11, OQ3; audit fix batch B5: I59): after `DIRECT DEPOSIT`, `ACCOUNTS PAYABLE`, `MISC PAYMENT` or `BILL PAYMENT` a reference of six to fifteen digits is no part of the payer region unless a province or territory (or a cut of one), Quebec's form or BMO's code tail (`AP`, `CC`, `MSP`, `DIV`, `APY`, `EDI`, `EED`) follows it; the template arm reads a number only when nothing, a province or that tail follows; both arms read six to ten digits. Scotiabank's and CIBC's named payers (an AP platform, suppliers, a town) reach Q6 and the accounts-payable remittance; BMO's fifteen-digit `DIRECT DEPOSIT <reference> MSP DIV` reads `no_payer_printed`. A name that starts with a province's cut (`NORTH …`, `NEW …`) still reads numbered (the safe direction). Measured: 108 rows, $274,483.18, counted → Verified (92 `named_payer_repeats`, 16 `payer_remittance_descriptor`); 3 rows, $10,570.00, relabelled `named_payer_one_off`; 93 BMO rows, $33,190.22, relabelled `no_payer_printed`; BMO's 173 genuine numbered rows unchanged; Scotiabank's 440 card settlements in the same slot stay Verified. True unchanged. 346881a.
- **American Express's incoming-ACH template is ACH** (§5.2 template prefixes and electronic-payment form, §8.2, §8.6, T13; audit fix batch B5: I19; standard A4, A16): `ONLINE TRANSFER PAYMENT CREDIT <originator> <entry> <id> [<receiver account>] <receiver> EXTERNAL <bank> ID <trace>` (the label at the row's start only) is no transfer wording and is an electronic-payment form and a template prefix (audit round 2, R2-B09: R31, the line below: only when an originator word follows the label; the bare label, or the label before `FROM`, an account word, `ID`, a masked account or digits, keeps its transfer reading and is held, Q2); the payer region is the originator and its entry before the id, and the receiver block, up to `EXTERNAL`, is a receiver field, so the receiver's masked account never fires T13's account designation and the holder's name there never fires a hold, while the holder test reads the originator (the holder's own pull stays held). The originator's own entry word `TRANSFER` still reads as transfer wording, as Bank of America's `DES:TRANSFER` does (a named transfer). Measured: 10 rows, $121,618.48, held → True (2 Verified by company form); 8 rows counted as `p2p` read as ACH payers (1 Verified by company form). 346881a.
- **Q5 reads an LLC the originator field cut** (§8.5's repeat arm; audit fix batch B5: I61, the cut-legal-form part only): on an N1 wire, a word after the sender's first that is `LL` glued to two or more letters, ending within four characters of the 35-character Fedwire and CHIPS originator field (`<name> MANAGEMENT LL<city>`), is a company form for Q5's repeat arm only; never a two-letter cut of another form (`CO`, `IN`, `LI`), never a name word that starts with `LL` earlier, never Q6's company form. Whether cut or whole trade words (`SER`, `INTERNATIO`, `SERVICES`) make a company for Q5 is owner decision I61. Measured: 4 rows of one statement, $71,850.00, counted → Verified (`repeat_wire_sender`). 346881a.
- **TD Canada Trust's Payments Canada codes** (§5.2 electronic-payment form, §8.6, §8.8, §8.9; audit fix batch B5: I37; Payments Canada Standard 007): on TD Canada Trust's statements only, a row whose last word is `AP` (460), `MSP` (450), `BPY` (430) or `APY` (439) prints an electronic-payment form, so Q6 reads its named payer, and a trailing `PAY` (the 200 family, payroll deposits) is pay wording (Q8: held as `payroll_or_salary`); `DIV` (250, a dividend) is neither; a US bank's trailing word is its own. The US layouts with no ACH word stay `named_payer_unlabelled` (owner decision I37). Measured: 34 rows, $171,086.34, counted → Verified; 18 rows relabelled `named_payer_one_off`; 8 `PAY` rows, $31,132.80, counted → held (the audit found most of them from an insurance group, which may be commission payroll; held until a person confirms). 346881a.
- **Navy Federal's and Symitar's payer is read past the digit block** (§5.2's ACH payer region; audit fix batch B5: I26's reader): `DEPOSIT ACH PAID FROM <digits> <payer>` and `EXTERNAL DEPOSIT <digits> <payer>` drop the block of four or more digits before the payer (a numbered company's number before its province is kept), so the holder, financing and insurer holds, the counter-debit holds and Q6's key read the payer, where every such payer had pooled under one layout key. How insurer spellings (`INS`) are held waits for owner decision I27. Measured: 2 rows of one statement, an insurer's credits beside the holder's own payment to that insurer, Verified → held as `payer_also_debited` (declared `q5_q6_held_counter_debit`; under I27 they may be agency commissions). 346881a.

- **Every IPFS credit spelling takes the insurance treatment** (§6.1 step 5; lender detection spec §4.2, amended; audit fix batch B6: I24): on a credit the premium-finance lookup reads a one-word alias of four letters or more with a digit run glued after it (`IPFS<office number>`, `IPFSFLP<code>`), and the ledger gains `IPFSFNDFLP` and `IPFSFLP`; a debit's lookup is unchanged. - **The financing roots are read only on text that can name the payer** (§8.1 `financing_named_payer`, T4's reading places and its `CREDIT` rule; amends T4 where it reads text that names no payer; audit fix batch B7: I16; Q2, Q3, Q4, Q5, the registry note that a bank's name is never the sender): a cash, branch, ATM or check deposit with no payer region is never read (its location: a city whose name holds `LEND`, `<n> CAPITAL BLVD`, `ATM DEPOSIT <NAME> FIN CU <street>`); a bank's branch label after a bank's name (`BOFA FIN CTR`), `MEMBER FDIC`, `EQUAL HOUSING LENDER`, a fundraising purpose and Wave Payments' payout entry (`WAVE <code> FUNDIN WAVE`, `WAVE FUNDING INSTRU…`) are removed first on every row; on a person-to-person rail row the sender's bank (a listed sending bank, the statement's bank, a credit union's name before `CREDIT UNION` or `FCU`) is removed and a sender that reads as a person's name is not read (a root inside a surname); on a wire's purpose text a buyer's `ADVANCE PAYMENT`, `ADVANCE PMT` or `PREPAYMENT` is removed; the plural `CREDITS` is no root, and a whole-word `CREDIT` ending the payer region after a single letter is a cut name's entry word (`<NAME> P CREDIT`). A custodian or a private bank (BNY Mellon, Pershing, Northern Trust) is never added: a customer's tuition paid from a brokerage account is still the customer's payment (the BNY Mellon transfer to a school stays counted, UNCONFIRMED I01/I28). Measured: 9 rows in 3 accounts, $119,242.07, leave True (8 wires and one ACH credit); one held Kraken credit is relabelled from `payer_also_debited`. 84c824f.
- **An HSA distribution and a cut annuity payment are personal income** (§8.1 `personal_income`; audit fix batch B8: I28): `HSA ?DISTRIB…` (a person's own medical savings) and a life insurer's annuity payment its sixteen-character originator field cut (`<insurer> LIFE AN DISB…`). Measured: 4 rows in 3 accounts, $3,640.15, leave True. 84c824f.
- **Legal settlements are held, and a settlement or closing agent is a title firm** (§8.1 `non_sale_purpose`, the A3 title-firm line above; audit fix batch B8: I44; the MCA principle: a legal windfall is no operating earning; amends nothing of §8.6, whose bare `SETTLEMENT` stays counted and never verified): `LAWSUIT`, `LITIGAT…`, `CLASS ACTION`, `DATA BREACH`, `SETTLEMENT ADMIN…`, the claims administrators (Epiq, JND Legal, `KCC` before `LLC`, `CLASS` or `SETTLEMENT`, Angeion, Simpluris, Rust Consulting, Kroll Settlement, A B Data, Verita) and a lawyer's or client trust account's payer (`IOLTA`, `ESQ`, `LAW`, `ATTORNEY` on the payer region) paying `SETTLEMENT PROCEEDS` or `FUNDS` are held for review (a law firm or a contractor may earn such money), unless the row pays a vendor's invoice; `<NAME> SETTLEMENT SERVICES` and `<NAME> CLOSING SERVICES` are title firms (property-closing proceeds), their fee payments counted as a title firm's are. A carrier's `CONTRACTOR MGMT SETTLEMENT` and a processor's settlement stay counted. Measured: 2 rows in 2 accounts, $4,208.12, leave True (a closing agent's wire and a data-breach settlement's person-to-person payout). 84c824f.
- **Equipment auctions' and car buyers' seller payouts are deferred sources** (§15, §19 OQ4; audit fix batch B8: I45; whether to hold them waits for the owner): the registry's `SECTION_15_DEFERRED_PAYERS` adds Ritchie Bros/RB Global, IronPlanet, Purple Wave, BigIron, GovDeals, Proxibid, `CARVANA PAYOUT`, CarMax (never CarMax Auto Finance, a listed auto lender), Peddle and Vroom, and Copart and IAA on a seller or consignor word only (they also pay their towing vendors), so Q5 and Q6 never verify them and on a wire or an ACH row their reason is `named_payer_purpose_refused`. Measured: one counted row's reason moves from `named_payer_one_off`; True and Verified unchanged. 84c824f.
- **Statement furniture is cut before classification; §6.3's narrowing 11** (§7.0, §6.3, §14.2; audit fix batch B8: I46; standard P14; a classifier guard, the parsers' own fixes waiting for audit batch B10): the revenue text cuts the furniture a parser glued onto a row (TD's footer and back panel, a Symitar credit union's balance, check-recap and fee summaries, a page header, Citizens' continued-page header and footer, Fifth Third's disclosure line, a page number), from its first form to the row's end, so its `LOAN`, `INTEREST EARNED` or `RETURNED ITEM` never decides a deposit; a row the cut would leave with no letter keeps its text; pass 7's counter-debit kinds read a debit past the same furniture. A deposit that leaves the strong non-revenue or reversal rule this way is §6.3's narrowing 11 (the census's `12_statement_furniture`, read on its masked text with a copy of the forms), and the census's standing sweep reads a True credit past the furniture too. Measured: 24 rows in 19 accounts move, True +$24,760.75 (TD mobile and bare deposits, a Venmo cash-out, a Stripe real-time payout, two Symitar check deposits, a Citizens Stripe transfer and two TD Intuit deposits now read as QuickBooks Payments' settlement form), Verified +$3,493.91 (4 rows); 11 rows leave `non_revenue` by the narrowing ($25,949.97), of which the prediction market's payout, a TD payroll deposit and a round-sum person-to-person row are held; the three bare CBUSOL transfers whose glued header printed an account number stay held, now as `transfer_no_counterparty`. 84c824f. Amended by audit round 2, R2-B10 (R21, below): the furniture adds Zions' legal-name footer before a page counter and Navy Federal's `JOINT OWNER(S)` header, and the cut's residual is recorded: a loan, refund or own-account transfer a row printed after a page break inside its own description would be dropped with the furniture (0 corpus rows; the census counts them).
- **The plural `TRANSFERS` is transfer wording** (§8.2, Q2; audit fix batch B8: I46; amends audit B2's I21 line, which left it for this batch): `\bTRANSFERS\b` (`EXTERNAL DEPOSIT ACH TRANSFERS PAYMENT`, `… PAY BY PHONE <account> FUNDS TRANSFERS`) reads as a transfer, held as `transfer_no_counterparty` (or `transfer_account_designation`) when it names no counterparty and read as a named transfer when it does; the holds' posting labels drop `FUNDS TRANSFERS` as they drop `FUNDS TRANSFER`. A Q5 or Q6 repeat it takes is the census's declared `q5_q6_bare_transfers`. Measured: 5 rows in 2 accounts, $34,804.92, leave True, of which 4 Verified rows of one credit union's account ($30,019.92) are demoted. 84c824f. Amended by audit round 2, R2-B10 (R21 part C, below): a row that starts with the plural wire label (`WIRE TRANSFERS FROM|INCOMING|DEPOSIT|IN|CREDIT`) is an incoming wire, never a person-to-person transfer; `TRANSFERS` stays transfer wording on every other row.
- **A glued Canada Carbon Rebate is held** (§8.1 `non_sale_purpose`; GOV class D, the owner's ruling of 2026-09-30; audit fix batch B8: I56): `CDA ?CARBON ?REBATE` with no left word boundary (TD Canada Trust's `CDACARBONREBATE CCR`) and `CANADA CARBON REBATE` are a government grant (`government_grant`); the spaced spelling was already held by its `REBATE` word. WSIB paying a clinic stays counted (revenue; the owner's class E). Measured: one row leaves True. 84c824f.
- **The GOV follow-ups** (§7.11 T5, §8.1 `personal_income`; the orchestrator's delegated decisions on the GOV ruling of 2026-09-30): the IRS's interest on a refund (`IRS TREAS 310 DES:INT`, `… INTEREST`, Chase's `CO ENTRY DESCR INT`) is `non_revenue` beside its refund forms (interest, standard G5; the census's IRS marker declares it), while `INTL` and the IRS's vendor entries keep their readings; RBC's cut label of the provincial payment code with the CRA's originator (`Prov./Loc.G.Paym Canada`, `… PRO`) is a personal benefit (class B, held as `personal_income`), as its spelled-out form is; with a province (`… ONTARIO`) or a company's name after it, it keeps its reading (class E). Measured: no corpus row prints either form. 84c824f.
- **An owner-shaped payer's `DISB` is owner money** (§8.1 `non_sale_purpose`; routed by the B7 review round 2 line above): the abbreviation `DISB` on an owner-shaped payer (`HOLDINGS`, `PARTNERS`, `PARTNERSHIP`, `LP`, `LLP`, `LLLP`, a venture, a family office, a trust or an estate) is held as its `DISBURSEMENT` is, however often it repeats; an ordinary payer's `CASH DISB` (its payment to a vendor) and a factor's `CASH DISB` keep their readings. Measured: no corpus row (no owner-shaped payer prints `DISB`). 84c824f.
- **The census for audit fix batch B8** (§14.2, §14.3): `NARROWINGS` gains `12_statement_furniture` (§6.3's narrowing 11, into True); `DECLARED_DEMOTIONS` and `LATER_HOLD_CAUSES` gain `q5_q6_held_gambling_payout`, `q5_q6_held_legal_settlement` and `q5_q6_held_owner_disbursement` (keyed on `non_sale_purpose` and the pass's new `hold_rule` readings `gambling_payout`, `legal_settlement`, `owner_disbursement`) and `q5_q6_bare_transfers` (a Q5 or Q6 row held as `transfer_no_counterparty` that prints `TRANSFERS`); the IRS's interest is the existing IRS marker; the standing sweep reads a True credit past the statement furniture. 84c824f.
- **Audit fix batch B8 in all** (B7 review round 2's tree to this one, input basis): True 68.735% → 68.674% (−$169,777.28, −0.089% of True), Verified 28.629% → 28.620% (−$26,526.01: 4 rows demoted, $30,019.92, declared `q5_q6_bare_transfers`; 4 rows promoted, $3,493.91), held 16.536% → 16.607%, non-revenue 14.729% → 14.719%; statements with $0 True 141 unchanged, with $0 Verified 1,079 → 1,080, gated 33.14% → 33.23%; the census compare holds every guard, with 11 rows leaving `non_revenue` by narrowing 11 and eligible deposits unchanged on 2,493 accounts; the standing sweep's non-revenue in True falls from $239,959.11 to $114,066.27 (its investment and crypto, gambling and government classes emptied); the walk moves 355 walked credit rows and no debit; G7 with the owner's allowlist: no lender gained or lost, 0 row-flag moves, eligible deposits and withholding unchanged on every walked input, the Factoring group's credits unchanged. 84c824f.
- **The holder's name is the statement's own field** (§5.2 holder words, §9.1, §9.3; audit fix batch B9: I13; amends the A3-G refinement's reading of `account_name` alone): `Statement.holder_name` holds the holder's name the parser read from the header or the mailing block, apart from the product, nickname or name `account_name` prints, and every classification record carries it; the holder tests (§5.2, §8.1, §7.9's wire vetoes, the recurrence pass's store-name words, the census's holder facts) read it first (`holder_reading`), the account-name field when none was read; `_business_account` and the account's business shape still read only the account-name field. Chase reads the first name line of its mailing block (after the mail-routing code line, its customer-service panel aside; a `DBA` line, a legal form or the rest of a name wrapped after a connector kept, an owner's or a contact's line never; a block flattened onto the code's line read up to its house number or post-office box; a returned-mail hold dropped) and never publishes its summary grid header (`INSTANCES AMOUNT`, `AMOUNT`, the header with the grid's first row glued on) as `account_name`; First Citizens reads the line above `Your Account(s) At A Glance` when it closes on a state and ZIP, without the address and without the account designation after the name's last legal form (`PREMIUM TRUST ACCOUNT`, `OPERATING ACCOUNT`, `WIRE ACCOUNT`, `PRODUCER PAYABLE ACCOUNT`, any other designation; a name with no legal form loses only a trailing designation that ends in `ACCOUNT`), each side of a DBA kept. The re-parse of every cached Chase and First Citizens reading the census reaches (537 analysis inputs, frozen code, deterministic, offline) reproduces every transaction byte for byte; only the two name fields move, and the corpus extraction gate stays GREEN (109 pinned PDFs; 102 validated, 3 review, 3 withheld, 1 refused, as at the base). `ANALYSIS_STAGE_VERSION` 25 → 26. Measured: 490 Chase and 39 First Citizens census accounts read a holder name; 43 credit rows in 24 accounts move to `holder_name` beside the self test's (True about −$116 thousand, −0.06% of True; Verified about −0.07% of Verified: six repeats that are one holder's own pushes from its other bank under its now-known name), every one reviewed: the holder's own name sending by Zelle, Cash App, a real-time or book transfer or ACH, an owner's own name, a near-identical name, and four rows already held that only change their reason. 8607e54.
- **The holder's own push and own wire are held on the row alone** (§8.1 `holder_name`; audit fix batch B9: I13; amends §7.9, whose own-account reading only refused Q5): an incoming wire whose sender's first two words are its beneficiary's, and an ACH or EFT credit whose originator is its own receiver field (`INDN`, `IND NAME`, `RECEIVER NAME`, `RECV NAME`; Capital One's unlabelled receiver after its originator, entry and digit run) by the census's class-B reading (equal, or one starting the other at a word with two words or more, or a cut of fifteen characters, the legal forms at the end aside except `CO` and `COMPANY`), are held as `holder_name`, whatever names the statement prints. A processor's or a platform's settlement or payout under the merchant's own name is no own push and takes no holder hold at all (`NET SETTLE`, `NETSWEEP`, `FINTECHEFT`, `BANKCARD`, `MERCH`, `MERCHANT`, `MERCHPAYOUT`, `MRCHNTSRVS`), nor is a payout whose entry leads with the originator's own first word (a statement descriptor, numbered names included); registry-claimed rows keep the holds' exemption. Measured: 11 ACH and EFT rows in 6 accounts leave True (about −$80 thousand, −0.04% of True; Verified about −0.09% of Verified, seven Mercury pushes, declared `q5_q6_held_holder_name`), and 11 Fedwires whose sender is their beneficiary, one holder's own (about −0.07% of True; two more already held change their reason); the standing sweep's class B (the holder's own ACH) falls from about $80 thousand to $0; Worldpay's `NET SETTLE` and Stripe's `NETSWEEP` rows are unchanged. 8607e54. Audit round 2, R2-B04 (R41, R04 part 3; amends this line, whose receivers were the labelled fields and Capital One's trailing one, and whose hold reached only rows no rule had verified): the receivers include American Express's unlabelled receiver block (B5's `ONLINE TRANSFER PAYMENT CREDIT` template, a `TRANSFER` entry included), paired with the originator and with the payer region less its last word; Capital One's pair is read without Mercury's `MERCURYACH` and `MRCR` tails; and a row it reads that an earlier rule verified (step 11's accounts-payable entries) is demoted by pass 7 to `holder_name` (method `self_originated_holder`) and, like every own push, counts toward no repeat (below).
- **First Citizens' DBA lump sums are the holder's own** (§5.2, §8.1; audit fix batch B9: I38; the owner's rule that the holder never counts, industry row B7): with the holder line read, a holder's name that prints a trade name is tested whole and as each of its two names, so the agency's own `<trade name> PY<date>` lump sums, printed under the trade name of the holder's premium trust account, are held as `holder_name`. Whether premium-trust credits count at all stays the owner's question (I39), and what the lump sums are stays unconfirmed. Measured: 4 counted rows leave True (about −$5.0 million, −2.63% of True, the brief's predicted increment of about $5.0M; the May lump sum and the agency's own were already held by B4's counter-debit hold and only change their reason), and one payroll row changes its reason. 8607e54.
- **The holder test reads names, not industry words** (§5.2; audit fix batch B9: I58; amends §5.2's first-distinctive-word arm, keeping its brand-word affiliate): when either name leads with a short initialism (three letters, after the name-starters and the particles `DEL`, `LOS`, `LAS`, `LES`, `VAN`, `VON`, `DOS`, `DAS`, `SAN`), the leading words are compared as printed (the same initialism is a likely affiliate, a different one a different company); a one-word holder only matches a payer whose name it leads; `BMO`, `ADV`, `BUS`, `CKG` and `GENERAL` are the holder test's noise on both sides (the payer, debit and lender readers keep `HOLDER_NOISE`, so no debit reading moves); and a payer name that prints another payer's name (two name words or more) and then a run of the holder's name reads without that run, its unlabelled receiver (a layout's labels before the holder's own name name no payer). Measured: one row returns to True (a single row: a Zelle from a `… GENERAL SERVICES INC` payer into an account named for its product); the sister-company and holding-company rows stay held; no other base hold is released. 8607e54.
- **The receiver fields are read beside a parser's holder name; a bare two-word fragment needs corroboration; `holder_unscreened`** (§5.2, §8.1, §9.1; audit fix batch B9: I13; amends the A3-G refinement's switch): the holder's names the statement's own receiver fields print are switched off only by an account name that names the holder with a legal form or three words or more, never by the parser's holder name (a bank that prints the owner as the receiver keeps the owner's own money held) and never by a bare two-word name; the account name names nobody when it is the summary grid header or a name of the holder test's bank and product abbreviations. `metrics.holder_unscreened` is true when no holder's name is known for the statement (no parser holder name, no account name that names a holder, no receiver-field holder name); audit batch B11 shows it on the True card and in the exports. Measured: 1,652 of 2,493 census accounts are holder-unscreened after this batch's Chase and First Citizens readers (Bank of America 246, Wells Fargo 220, Royal Bank of Canada 158, TD Canada Trust 76, Scotiabank 71). 8607e54. Audit round 2, R2-B04 (R19; amends this line's `holder_unscreened`, which the code read as the holder's name failing `_usable_holder_name`): a holder's name the parser read is known whenever the holder test reads a word of it, whatever its shape, as the holder hold reads it; an account-name field is known when it names a holder (`_usable_holder_name`, which now also reads a trailing `CO`, `S.E.N.C.`, a `DBA`, `AKA` or `Operating as` side, a leading `THE` and a partnership's `AND`); and a statement with no transaction is read by its own names (below).
- **The census for audit fix batch B9** (§14.2, §14.3): the guard facts `holder`, `own_transfer` and `wire_vetoed` read the holder's name as the pass does (`holder_reading`, on a checkout that has it), and `holder` is false on a processor's settlement or payout under the merchant's own name (`merchant_settlement`), which the pass may verify (without it G3 read 143 Stripe `NETSWEEP` rows under the merchant's now-known name as failures). The new holds of a Q5 or Q6 row are the declared `q5_q6_held_holder_name`. 8607e54.
- **Audit fix batch B9, Chase and First Citizens** (B8's tree to this one, input basis, the Chase and First Citizens readings re-parsed with the frozen tree): True 68.674% → 66.749% (−$5,328,728.60, −2.80% of True, −1.92 points; the brief predicted about −$5.2M and about −1.8 points), Verified 28.620% → 28.573% (−$129,836.87: 13 rows demoted, all declared `q5_q6_held_holder_name`; none promoted), held 16.607% → 18.531%, non-revenue 14.719% unchanged; statements with $0 True 141 unchanged, with $0 Verified 1,080 → 1,081, median statement True 82.5% → 82.1%, gated 33.23% → 33.67%; the census compare holds every guard, with no row leaving `non_revenue` and eligible deposits unchanged on 2,493 accounts; the walk moves 129 walked credit rows and no debit; G7 with the owner's allowlist: no lender gained or lost, 0 row-flag moves, eligible deposits and withholding unchanged on every walked input. 8607e54.
- **TD Bank, Truist, PNC, Navy Federal and TD Canada Trust read the holder's name too** (§5.2 holder words, §9.1, §9.3; audit fix batch B9: I13, the brief's other parsers that publish a product as `account_name`): TD Bank reads its header's `<holder> Account # <number>` line (never `Primary Account #:`, never a row after the summary); Truist the name after its mail-routing code line; PNC the words in capitals before the product on its summary title line (`<HOLDER> <Product> Summary Account number`); Navy Federal the first line of the mailing block under `Questions about this Statement?`, its toll-free panel aside; TD Canada Trust the line after its `TDCDA…` mail-routing code, closing on a province and a postal code, on both of its entry points (the native one now wraps the row reader, whose frozen oracle in tests/test_row_grouping.py is unchanged). A contact after the name (`ATTN`, `ATTENTION`, `C/O`) or a court case and its number is no part of it; a numbered company keeps its number; `account_name` keeps each parser's product. The pinned corpus's 108 published statements keep every extracted field (period, balances, counts, each transaction's date, description, amount, direction and running balance), only the two name fields moving, and the corpus extraction gate stays GREEN. The cached readings read a holder name on 40 of 42 TD Bank, 51 of 51 Truist, 42 of 45 PNC, 46 of 52 Navy Federal and 85 of 85 TD Canada Trust readings (the misses print no anchor). The extraction schema's pin moves with the field (tests/test_extraction_schema_prompt_surface.py). 1c94ff3.
- **The holder's name inside another payer's receiver block is never the payer** (§5.2; amends this batch's trailing-run reading, 8607e54, which read the run only where it ended the row): Truist's company field runs on into the receiver block, the holder's name cut or followed by a person's, so a run of the holder's first two name words after two name words or more of another payer is read with everything after it removed. 1c94ff3.
- **Audit fix batch B9, the other five parsers** (8607e54's tree to this one; the seven banks' readings re-parsed with each frozen tree on both sides: those five banks' cached analyses predate their current parsers, so on this basis 2,504 census accounts reconcile): 56 credit rows in 22 accounts move to `holder_name` (True −$27,312.13, −0.015% of True; Verified unchanged, no row demoted), every one reviewed: the holder's own Zelles, Cash App transfers and wires under its name, a sister company of the holder's brand, and rows already held that only change their reason; but 13 of the Truist rows were platform payouts read as the holder's own because Truist prints the holder's name in its receiver slot after the payer (a retailer marketplace's automatic cash-outs after its one-word company field, a booking platform's payouts, and a live-shopping marketplace's payout whose company field prints the store's descriptor: True about −$3.4 thousand of this move), which review round 1 restores (fe1ed9d, below); two rows remain held on two shared industry words of another company's name (residual, reported for review; held is the safe direction). True 66.754% → 66.745%, Verified 28.551% unchanged, held 18.527% → 18.537%; statements with $0 True 141 → 142, gated 33.68% → 33.76%; the census compare holds every guard, eligible deposits unchanged on 2,504 accounts; the walk moves 57 walked credit rows and no debit; G7 with the owner's allowlist: no lender gained or lost, 0 row-flag moves. `holder_unscreened`: 1,434 of 2,504 census accounts (Bank of America 246, Wells Fargo 220, Royal Bank of Canada 158, Scotiabank 71). 1c94ff3.
- **A processor's settlement word is read outside the holder's own names** (§8.1 `holder_name`; audit fix batch B9 review round 1: I13; amends 8607e54's exemption, which read the words anywhere on the row before its receiver fields): `NET SETTLE`, `NETSWEEP`, `FINTECHEFT`, `BANKCARD`, `MERCH`, `MERCHANT`, `MERCHPAYOUT` and `MRCHNTSRVS` exempt a row from the holder holds only where they print outside the holder's own names (the parser's holder name, an account-name holder beside it, each side of a DBA name, the receiver-field holder names; for the row-alone test, the row's own receiver and originator, so a self push whose name prints one of the words is held on the row alone too), and never on an incoming wire or a person-to-person or push rail, which print no processor's settlement. A holder whose name prints one of the words (`NORTHWIND MERCHANT GROUP LLC`, a sole proprietor surnamed Merchant) keeps its own Zelle, push and wire held. The exemption still covers every holder arm, not only the row-alone test, because Stripe's `NETSWEEP` and a beverage payment platform's `FINTECHEFT` print the merchant's own name as their originator: gating the self test alone would hold about 140 Q6-verified rows of the walked inputs and 5 counted ones that are processor settlements. The census's holder fact reads the pass's own reading of the payer region (`true_revenue.region_names_holder`: the holder's names, the layout's receiver block, the settlement words), so G3 reads what the hold reads. Measured: no walked row moves (no holder name the corpus reads prints the words). fe1ed9d.
- **The row's layout says where another payer's receiver block can print** (§5.2; audit fix batch B9 review round 1: I13; amends 8607e54's and 1c94ff3's trailing-run reading, which needed two name words of another payer on every layout): never inside a labelled sender, which prints the sender alone (an incoming wire's originator field, a person-to-person or push rail, a transfer's sender after `FROM`), so an owner's name printed before the holder's (`ZELLE PAYMENT FROM SAMPLE PERSON NORTHWIND MARINE LLC`) is the holder's own money, as before B9; after one name word on Truist's receiver slot (`<entry> <company field> <receiver> CUSTOMER ID <id>`, the company field unpadded; read on any row printing `CUSTOMER ID` until review round 2, below, read it on a Truist statement only), so a one-word originator's payout (`TRANSFER FABRIKAM INC CONTOSO GAMES CUSTOMER ID …`) and a platform whose company field prints the store's descriptor are never the holder's; two name words on any other row, as before (one would release the holder's own money printed after an entry or rail word on PNC's and Navy Federal's layouts: 6 walked rows). Measured: 13 Truist credit rows in 5 census accounts leave `holder_name` (True about +$3.4 thousand, +0.0018% of True; Verified unchanged): a retailer marketplace's automatic cash-outs (8) and a live-shopping marketplace's payout (1) are counted again, and of a booking platform's 4 payouts one is counted, two stay held as a round-sum repeat and one (under $1) as a non-sale purpose; no labelled-sender row moves. fe1ed9d.
- **The wire hold compares the whole names** (§8.1 `holder_name`; audit fix batch B9 review round 1: I13; amends 8607e54's hold, which reused §7.9's first-two-words test): an incoming wire is held as the holder's own only when its sender's and its beneficiary's whole names, each read up to its first digit token (the address's number or ZIP) and cut after its first legal form (review round 2, below: a name no legal form ends is read without its town and state too), are one by the class-B reading; §7.9's Q5 veto keeps the two-word reading, so `THE SAMPLE COMPANY INC` wiring `THE SAMPLE BAKERY LLC` is counted and refused as proof, never held. Measured: no walked row moves (every sender-equals-beneficiary wire the corpus prints is one name). fe1ed9d.
- **PNC never reads its own product as the holder** (§9.3 `statement.holder_name`; audit fix batch B9 review round 1: I13; amends 1c94ff3's PNC reader): a summary title whose capitals are only product words (`TREASURY ENTERPRISE PLAN`, `ANALYSIS BUSINESS CHECKING`, `PNC CORE COMMERCIAL CHECKING`) names no holder, and a product printed in capitals after the name is cut off. No cached PNC reading changes its holder name; the pinned corpus's statements keep every extracted field (holder names included) and the corpus extraction gate stays GREEN, its report equal to the base's; `ANALYSIS_STAGE_VERSION` stays 26 (the batch's one bump; stage 26 was never published). fe1ed9d.
- **An account-name holder is read beside the parser's** (§5.2 holder words; audit fix batch B9 review round 1: I13; amends 8607e54's `holder_reading` for the holder hold and the census's holder fact): beside a parser's `holder_name`, an `account_name` that names a holder (a person or a company, never a product; this line's reading let PNC's `Treasury Enterprise Plan` through, which prints no noise word, until review round 2, below) is a second name of the holder's, as each side of a DBA name is (`_holder_names`), so a layout that prints the owner there keeps the owner's own money held. Measured: no walked row moves (the seven readers leave a product, nothing or the holder in `account_name`). fe1ed9d.
- **A one-word holder left by a dropped lead word is read with that word** (§5.2; audit fix batch B9 review round 1: I58; amends 8607e54's one-word arm under `GENERAL` as noise): when the holder test drops the word a one-word holder's name leads with (`GENERAL`, a two-letter initialism), the payer must print the holder's run from that word (`GENERAL PLUMBING LLC` holds `GENERAL PLUMBING`, never `PLUMBING SUPPLY HOUSE INC`; `JB TRUCKING LLC` never holds `TRUCKING PARTNERS INC`); a one-word holder printed alone or after a bank's or a product's abbreviation keeps the one-word reading. Measured: no walked row moves. fe1ed9d.

- **A product a holder reader publishes never names the holder** (§5.2 holder words; audit fix batch B9 review round 2: I13; amends fe1ed9d's account-name holder, whose `_usable_holder_name` rejected only a product printing a noise word, and 8607e54's holder reading): an account name with no company form whose holder-test words are all the readers' product words beyond the noise (`classifier.account_product`: PNC's `PNC`, `CORE`, `VIRTUAL`, `WALLET`, `WITH`, `SPEND`, `TREASURY`, `ENTERPRISE`, `PLAN`) names nobody. It is never a second holder name beside the parser's (`_usable_holder_name`), and as the only holder reading it has no holder words and holds no payer (`holder_words`, `holder_named`), so PNC's `Treasury Enterprise Plan` never holds the U.S. Treasury's `US TREASURY 310` payments (PNC's corporate-ACH form included) or `TREASURY SAMPLE GROUP INC`, which verify on repeat; a company printing those words with a legal form (`TREASURY SAMPLE PLAN LLC`) still names its holder. Every other product the seven readers publish already named nobody beside a holder name (each prints a noise word); TD Canada Trust's `CHEQUING` stays a holder word when the product is the only reading (unchanged: another reader's bare `CHEQUING` account name holds the holder's own transfers from its chequing account). - **The one-word receiver slot is Truist's** (§5.2; audit fix batch B9 review round 2: I13; amends fe1ed9d's receiver block, which read one name word on any row printing `CUSTOMER ID`): the slot is read on a Truist statement only (the statement's bank), so another bank printing `CUSTOMER ID` keeps the two-word reading; and a company field printing the holder's own name glued (`TRANSFER CONTOSOGAMES CONTOSO GAMES CUSTOMER ID …`: whole, with its legal form glued on, or cut to twelve letters or more) is the holder's own money, held, never another payer's receiver block. Measured: no walked row moves (fe1ed9d's 13 Truist payouts stay counted or held as before; no other bank's `CUSTOMER ID` row moved then). ebd4ba3.
- **A wire party with no legal form is read without its town and state** (§8.1 `holder_name`; audit fix batch B9 review round 2: I13; amends fe1ed9d's wire hold, which read each name up to its first digit token, so a name with no legal form kept its town): a sender or beneficiary name that no legal form ends drops the town and state its address prints after it (one town word before a state's code, a country code after the state included) when two name words stay, so the holder's own wire from `NORTHWIND MARINE SAMPLEVILLE TX` to `NORTHWIND MARINE OTHERTOWN TX`, or a sole proprietor's, is held again as 8607e54 held it, while `NORTHWIND MARINE SUPPLY` wiring `NORTHWIND MARINE REPAIR` and fe1ed9d's lookalikes stay two and counted. A two-word town leaves its first word, which a one-word town's reading still starts; two two-word towns stay apart. Measured: no walked row moves. ebd4ba3.

- **A payer the parser did not read is 'payer text not read', never Q2's 'no payer printed'** (§5.3, §8.2, §8.10, §9.1; audit fix batch B10: I05, I60; amends §8.2's bare-deposit reading of a label a parser made): a deterministic parser marks a credit it publishes with only its own posting-type or section label because it could not read the payer the statement printed (`Transaction.payer_text_unread`, written only where set and kept out of the extraction response schema); where pass 7 would read such a row as a bare deposit it is `counted_flagged` / `payer_text_not_read` (label "Payer text not read"), never Verified; every hold before it still applies. The metrics gain `payer_text_unread` (more than 30% of the statement's posted credit rows marked) and `payer_text_unread_count`. Holding such rows instead is the owner's (it would change this section's rule). Measured: 11 credit rows in 3 statements are marked (the counted core-processor template's welded merchant blocks), all counted before and after (about $0.59 million moves from `no_payer_printed` to `payer_text_not_read`, no True change); the 3 statements carry `payer_text_unread`. First Citizens marks its `Other Credit` section label where its description overlay fails closed or is missing (its deposits grid prints no description column, so `Deposit` stays Q2's): latent, no cached First Citizens reading fails closed. 7ac84c8.
- **BMO US keeps a deposit's payer** (§5.2 payer region; audit fix batch B10: I05; amends the activity-grid reader, which published the posting type alone): a credit's description is its label, the text its own line prints after the amount (the Balance cell aside: `RTP CREDIT <payer> INST ID=…`) and the Description column's lines below it (`CCD <originator> <entry>`, `ZELLE FROM <sender>`, a card credit's merchant), past the record and card numbers and the page furniture a page break reprints; a withdrawal keeps its label until the owner admits the debit readings (G7). Measured (census accounts): 558 credit rows read their payer; 446 move in 21 accounts, True about +$37 thousand, Verified about +$220 thousand: customer Zelle held as a bare transfer is counted person-to-person (101 rows, about $36 thousand; 17 more are held as round-sum repeats), processor payouts by real-time payment, merchant settlements and processor ACH payouts verify (about 300 rows), and card credits stay held as possible returns (B3). 7ac84c8.
- **The counted core-processor template keeps the block below a credit's amount** (§5.2; audit fix batch B10: I05; amends `tables.attributed_descriptions`, which read nothing printed below an amount): on a credit section the flush printed below a row's amount is read by the same count as the span flush (`below`); it is the row's own when that row alone is outstanding and the flush is one line holding at most one ACH class code (`CCD`, `CTX`, `PPD` …), since the layer welds several rows' blocks onto one line and can flush the blocks of rows printed below; any other flush is published on no row and marks the rows outstanding at it as 'payer text not read'. Measured: 13 credit rows read their originator, 10 verify (about +$137 thousand) and one is a one-off; 11 rows in welded flushes are marked; no True change. The audit's estimate of about $0.36 million of lender money leaving True rested on attributing those welded flushes, which the count cannot prove; they are counted and marked instead. 7ac84c8.
- **The community platform keeps a credit's text after its amount, on its printed line** (§5.2; audit fix batch B10: I05, I14; amends `community_section._dated_amount_rows`, which published only the text before the amount): a credit's description is its label and the rest of the row's own printed line after the amount, up to the next row's date or a section trailer (the platform reader finds the row again in the document's printed lines, since the page's furniture follows on lines of its own); a debit keeps its label (G7). Falcon Bank's `International IWT` is an incoming-wire label (amends audit fix batch B5's I14 line, which kept IWT's reading until this batch), and its sender is read after the bank's own wire field (`… Falcon Internati Wires <sender>`, for `Incoming Wire Trf` too); a bare IWT names no sender. Measured: Arbor's 55 credit rows move (True about −$50 thousand, Verified about +$234 thousand: a job-management platform's and an accounting platform's payouts verify; two financing-named payers and an own-account transfer are held); Falcon's 7 incoming wires (about $68 thousand) leave `wire_no_sender` and are counted, its 30 IWT wires stay counted with the printed sender read, and two online transfers and a refund leave True; other issuers' 37 rows move (True about −$21 thousand, Verified about +$7 thousand). 7ac84c8.
- **The balance-by-date template keeps a transfer's own field** (§5.2; audit fix batch B10: I05; amends `_grid_rows`, which attributed no continuation text): on a credit section, a `TRNSFR CREDIT` row reads the `OLB XFER FROM <four digits>` field the line below prints when that line, printing no row of its own, holds exactly as many such fields as its row line holds such rows (left to right); any other count attributes nothing. Measured: 29 rows read the field and stay held (`transfer_no_counterparty`, B2's channel code). 7ac84c8.
- **U.S. Bank keeps an incoming wire's `ORG=` sender** (§8.5; audit fix batch B10: I12; amends the U.S. Bank reader, which dropped the lines below a credit's amount): a credit reads on from an `ORG=` field below its amount (the sender's name wrapping onto the next line with the start of its address); nothing else printed after a credit's amount is kept. - **'Payer text not read' replaces only §8.10's bare-row fall-through** (§8.2, §8.10 step 25; audit fix batch B10 review round 1: I05; amends 7ac84c8's swap, which also replaced step 16's reason): a marked row on the cash, branch, teller or ATM channel (`ATM DEPOSIT`, `TELLER DEPOSIT`) keeps `no_payer_printed`, since its own label says the statement printed no payer. - **A repeated statement is left out of every package figure** (§9.2, §10.1; audit fix batch B11: I06, MCA standard §4.6(3); amends D-3's surfacing, which flagged a repeated content hash and summed it): before any member is expanded, a job that repeats an earlier job's id (listed once), content hash (`same_content`) or, account by account, statement identity (`same_statement`: an account's content digest where its ledger prints a transaction, or its statement key where bank, account last four, period and opening and closing balance are all printed) is left out of every total and of the month count, listed in `documents` after the members with `is_duplicate`, `duplicate_of` and `duplicate_reason` and no figure, and the package goes to review; an account of a kept document that an earlier job published is excluded as `duplicate_statement`, its sibling accounts kept (the accounts of one document are never compared); `?ids=` and `POST /package` list each job once; the package page names the repeats and the upload queue a file repeated within its batch. Measured (the audit's probe, one fresh payload): the same PDF twice, a re-download and the same job listed twice no longer double True or add a month (True and months as the one statement's), and each sends the package to review. f97e607.
- **Months are calendar months, and the package publishes True per month** (§9.2, §10.1, §10.8; audit fix batch B11: I07, I33; amends MINOR-10's member count and the package's averages): `months` is the number of distinct calendar months the counted statements stand for (the Monthly Statements' rule), `statement_periods` published beside it where they differ; `avg_monthly_true_revenue`, `avg_monthly_verified_revenue` and `avg_monthly_non_revenue` are the package's totals over it, `None` where True is N/A and refused with the other per-period averages (one document's accounts, a mixed or undeclared currency); the dashboard prints "N statements · M months", True per month on the hero and two per-month cards, labels the gross monthly figure gross, and both copied summaries carry True, Verified and Non-revenue in total and per month. Measured (the audit's probe): two different accounts uploaded for one month read 1 month (was 2) and their averages are no longer halved. f97e607.
- **The review gate reads held credits against True plus held** (T7; audit fix batch B11: I50; amends T7's posted-credit base, which heavy non-revenue diluted): a statement or package member also needs revenue review when its held credits are 10% or more of True plus held, in integer cents, and at least $500 (`REVENUE_REVIEW_HELD_FLOOR`, so a $0-True statement never gates on held cents); the posted-credit share still gates on its own. Measured (2,493 census accounts): 39 more accounts gated (824 → 863, 33.39% → 34.97% of statements; the brief expected about 33), none with True $0, held 10.4% to 76.7% of their True plus held (2.2% to 9.9% of their posted credits). f97e607.
- **The tier identity has its own name, and "Balanced" means reconciled** (§9.1, §9.2, §10.1, T7; audit fix batch B11: I31): the four tiers summing to the posted credits is `revenue_tiers_sum_to_posted` (statement metrics, package totals, document rows), `revenue_reconciles` its deprecated alias; the Revenue chip and status row read "Balanced" only where `reconciliation.ok` and `revenue_established` hold (a package: every counted member reconciles, `unreconciled_documents` empty, revenue established, currencies not refused); the package decision no longer reads the identity (a member failing it gates through T7). f97e607.
- **The downloads leave unestablished figures empty** (§10.4, §10.8; audit fix batch B11: I51; amends §10.8's flattening, which printed the figures the dashboard reads as N/A): a statement download nulls True, Counted, Held and every alias where `revenue_established` is false and Verified and its aliases where `verified_revenue_established` is false; a package download nulls `maximum_revenue`, `potential_revenue_total`, `unverified_deposit_total` and `revenue_review_items` when a member is not established, and each document row's Verified and counted-plus-held figures under its flags; the served payloads are unchanged; the Non-Revenue card says "Captured rows only" where revenue is not established. Measured (590 fresh payloads): the 2 unestablished accounts' 16 numeric partial cells go to 0. f97e607.
- **The downloads are auditable row by row** (§10.8; audit fix batch B11: I32, I52; amends §10.8's per-state evidence sections and the inline-string sheet): the statement CSV/XLSX add one row per posted credit (date, description, amount, currency, tier, reason, state, review status); the package's evidence is one section per tier, each record carrying `source_currency` and `source_account`, and its combined ledger is written one posting a row; XLSX cells are typed (numbers numeric, booleans boolean; NaN, inf and numeric-looking text stay text, and text keeps its formula guard's literal apostrophe). f97e607.
- **The dashboard copy says what each figure is** (§5.3, §10.5, §10.6; audit fix batch B11: I53, I34 copy, B9's I13, B10's I05): the Counted note names every counted kind and the missing proof of the sale, the Verified note claims confirmed platform payouts only, Counted is broken down by reason in the evidence table, the credit mix says its shares are of posted credits, `payout_credit_entity` reads "BNPL or marketplace settlement (payer also lends)", the Confirmed Withholding placeholder names eligible deposits and the Eligible Deposits card on the legacy basis "Gross deposits less lender funding (not True revenue)" (the basis itself is the owner's I34), and the True card names an unscreened holder and unread payer text. f97e607.
- **A mixed-currency chart is scaled within each currency** (§10.3; audit fix batch B11: I54): a package that refuses to combine its currencies (several, or one beside an undeclared member) scales its bars and picks its peak within each currency, and every bar names its unit, USD included. f97e607.
- **Audit fix batch B11, in all** (c647e3f's tree to f97e607; the cached analysis inputs read as they are, since no parser changes; 2,493 census accounts): True 68.52%, Verified 28.59%, held 16.76%, $0-Verified 1,081 and $0-True 141 statements, all unchanged; no credit's state, tier or reason moves and no account's tier totals; gated 33.39% → 34.97% (T7 above); the census compare holds every guard (no demotion, nothing leaves `non_revenue`, eligible deposits unchanged on 2,493 accounts); the whole walk (7,241 inputs, 901,548 rows) moves no row; G7 with the owner's allowlist: no lender gained or lost, 0 row-flag moves, eligible deposits unchanged on 7,241 inputs and withholding unchanged on 7,176 (65 not comparable). `ANALYSIS_STAGE_VERSION` 27 → 28 (§9.3). f97e607.
- **The contract documents describe the code as built** (§5.3, §6.2, §11, T2, T7, T8, §8.1 and §18.2 item 7; `docs/transaction-classification.md` and `README.md`; audit fix batch B12: I62; no behavior changes): the classification contract now says that the interest, card-reward and reimbursement forms the strong rules name are `non_revenue` while the residual payer-less forms (a bare `INTEREST`, `INTEREST RECEIVED` or `MONTHLY INTEREST`, a credit union's bare `DIVIDEND`, a bare `REWARDS`, an entry code `REIMB…`, and a wire or transfer purpose printing such a word) are held as `non_sale_purpose`; that `unverified_deposit_total` is counted plus held and only the held part needs review; that `WITHHOLDING_DENOMINATOR_MODE` selects `verified_revenue` or `true_revenue`, neither the default, and that in either revenue mode `eligible_deposits` carries the selected denominator (the legacy figure stays in `legacy_eligible_deposits`), with the package's deductions and its partial-denominator rule; that the statement passes run as §6.2 numbers them, the labels for unnamed potential payees, pass 6 and pass 7 included; and it repeats `REVENUE_TIER_REASONS` as a table, with each batch's review decisions through this one.
