Read against s57 itself (the cache as it was, 2,493 accounts) True is 68.783% → 66.779%, and that compare's G1 and G7b failures are the cache's stage drift on the re-parsed inputs, which the same-basis compare above cancels: on 4 Navy Federal statements today's reader, s57's included, prints other descriptions on 86 credits (same indexes, amounts and directions) than the cached parse did, 43 of them debit-shaped, a drift that predates the fix run and that no fix batch changed. B12.
- **B12 review round 1: §17.1 and the contract corrected against the code** (§17.1 and this section's B12 lines; `docs/transaction-classification.md`; audit fix batch B12's review; no behavior changes, so no census move): §17.1's I18 and I45 line said both stay counted and flagged, but a builder's `DRAW` is held as `non_sale_purpose` by §8.1's financing words that carry no root (B7's I18 line above); only the equipment auctions' and car buyers' seller payouts are counted and flagged, never verified (`named_payer_purpose_refused`), and the line now says so. In the contract, the `insurer_named_payer` row names every word the hold reads (`INSUR…`, `ASSURANCE`, `MUTUAL`, `CASUALTY`, `INDEMNITY`, `UNDERWRIT…`) and its claim-word arm; the `unverified_deposit_total` entry says that the held total feeds `revenue_review_required` and that `revenue_review_items` is the held-row count, not a gate input; and the withholding paragraph says that no run or deploy file in this repository sets `WITHHOLDING_DENOMINATOR_MODE` (the tryout host's environment is the `fly.toml` on its deploy branch), in place of a claim about the host this repository cannot show. The docs test now parses the contract's reason table and compares it with `REVENUE_TIER_REASONS` row by row in its order, reads the insurer words from `_INSURER_NAME`, checks §19.1's five decision lines by their lead-ins and their words, scans the repository's run and deploy files for the withholding mode, and classifies four fabricated rows (a builder's `DRAW` entry and wire purpose, the same wire without the word, an equipment auction's seller payout) against §17.1's line. B12 review round 1.
- **The census keeps a parse's moves apart from a rule's, and reads a re-parsed basis** (§14.2 and §14.3, which gain G18, and this section's B1 line; audit round 2, R2-B01: R12, R30; census tooling only: no tier rule, classifier, parser, registry or debit changes): each credit records its raw parse (a hash of its digit-masked printed description and its cents) and each account its parse over every row, credits and debits (hashlib digests, the same in every process). `compare` pairs a statement's credits by position; a credit whose raw parse changed, or that has no partner, moved with the parse: it is compared apart (the move matrix of the rows read twice) and read only by the guards that read the later row alone, never by G1, G7b or a demotion cause, while a credit whose raw parse is unchanged is a rule's move as before (G7b keeps reading a change of its normalized text); a compare with a snapshot written before this batch pairs every credit as before. The new guard G18 fails an account whose parse changed (a moved credit, or a changed parse over every row) unless an explanation names it (`--explained-parse`): a note of the source check for that account, or, only when both snapshots were re-parsed, its parser route; the report lists each such account's bank, route, rows, dollars and the True delta of its moved rows, by bank. The snapshot keeps the filter of §14.2 and summarises the unreconciled accounts apart (count, banks, posted credits, tiers), and `compare` lists the accounts that reconcile in one snapshot only, both ways, with their True. `snapshot --reparse`, in this census and the lender census, re-parses every latest analysis input a cached OCR reading reaches with the checkout's deterministic analysis stage (no model) and reads the rest from the cache, counting both; it is the census's primary basis from this batch on. The census half of I60 (R30): the pdf basis counts the orphans newer than the statement it kept (`orphan_accounts_newer_than_kept`), a statement whose newest analysis cannot be read counts one error skip and is never replaced by an older parse (one replaced by a newer analysis counts none), and a set-aside analysis's parse change reads every row (`parse_sha`), not its credits alone. Measured with the frozen tool: on round 1's snapshots every compare report equals the base tool's but for the added keys (s57r → final still True 66.779%, Verified 28.694%, every guard held); the cached bases re-read with the new tool equal round 1's to the row (input 2,493 accounts, unreconciled 169; pdf 2,493 accounts, orphans matching a kept statement 3,636, newer than kept 0, no error skip). The re-parse reaches 2,554 of 7,241 inputs (2,550 re-parsed, 4 refused and read from the cache). The round-2 baseline (the whole re-parse at this code) holds 2,513 accounts, 5 more than round 1's final census (the 5 statements that reconcile only when re-parsed; posted credits +$0.23 million, True +$0.09 million, held +$0.10 million), unreconciled 154 → 149; True 66.779% → 66.755% and Verified 28.694% → 28.677% (a basis change, not a rule's); no other row moves. Against the cached basis (B1's frozen s57 code, as round 1's B12 compared it) the 43 G1 and 26 G7b failures leave those guards: they are R03's Navy Federal drift (4 accounts, 86 credits), now G18's, with one TD Canada Trust re-index and the 58 accounts of B10's parser routes whose credits keep their text and gain the payer after it (B10's other 3 accounts, with 17 more statements, reconcile only when re-parsed: 20 reconcile flips); each of the 63 is explained by a note, and every guard then holds. e94051b.
- **A platform's payout is known by its payout id** (§8.1 `holder_name` and `payer_also_debited`, §14.3 G3's holder fact; audit round 2, R2-B02: R18; standard A1, A2, B1, B7; amends B9's line above, whose exemption read an entry field leading with the originator's first word): `processor_payout` reads a platform's payout id in the ACH id field (Bank of America's `ID:ST-<id>`, Chase's `IND ID:ST-<id>`, ten characters or more, holding letters and digits since review round 1 below), never on an incoming wire or a person-to-person or push rail. It replaces the entry-word exemption in the row-alone self test (so the holder's own push whose entry echoes its name, `NORTHWIND MARINE DES:NORTHWIND ID:<digits> INDN:NORTHWIND MARINE LLC`, is held as `holder_name` on the row alone, and the corpus's one self-shaped Stripe payout stays counted), lifts the pass-7 holder hold (its region, sender and receiver-name arms) and the census's holder fact (`region_names_holder`), so a payout printing the merchant's descriptor as originator and entry is never held as the holder's money once a holder name is read; and a payout beside the platform's own pull printing a payout id (Stripe's refund and negative-balance pulls under the same descriptor) is not held as `payer_also_debited` unless the pull is for the payout's own amount. It lifts holds only and never verifies (a platform also pays claims, treasury and capital forms; `STRIPE CAPITAL` stays out of True). The hold order, claimed rows, debit kinds and tiers are unchanged. Measured with R01 below (the payout-id rows overlap): 44 rows, about $55 thousand, leave `payer_also_debited` in 6 accounts (the 34 descriptor payouts R01 lists, Verified, and 10 transfer-worded descriptor payouts of an invoicing platform, 4 counted as `p2p` and 6 sub-dollar rows held by the floor; the eleventh, beside a pull for its own amount, stays held); `holder_name` stays at 412 rows and the holder fact at 390 credits. 8be3257.
- **A processor's payout is counted beside its processor's own non-circular debits** (§8.1 `payer_also_debited`, this section's B4 line; audit round 2, R2-B02: R01; standard A1, §4.2; amends B4's "a processor's own fee … never holds a credit that prints settlement wording", which needed both the fee word and the settlement word before the receiver): a credit is a processor's or a platform's payout when it prints settlement wording or a payout form before its receiver fields (`MRCHNT SVCS`/`MRCHNTSVCS`, `MRCH SVCS`, `COMB DEP`/`COMBINED DEPOSIT`, Payment Brands' printed cuts `PAYMNTBRAND` and `PYMNTBRDMIN`, `INTUIT CANADA PAYMENTS` and its cut `INTUIT CANADA P`, `DEPOSIT <bank> MERCHANT`, a twelve-character payout id of letters and digits after `ST`) or a merchant-ID field anywhere on the row (`MID <six digits or more>`, which Chase and Truist print after the receiver). Such a payout is held only by a circular or lender debit, a debit for its own amount, or a non-card debit recurring at one amount: never by the processor's card charge or a one-off debit of its own that reads no fee or bill word (a subscription, `CARD PMT`, a debit printing only the processor's name, its merchant ID and digits). A card purchase in a form the card-form prefix misses (`DEBIT CARD PURCHASE`, `DBCRD PUR`, `POS DB`, `POS DEBIT`, `POS PURCHASE`, `POINT OF SALE`, a glued `PURCHASE<digits>`, a charge ending `<ST> USA` or `<ST> CAN`, or one the classifier classes `pos`) reads the kind `card` where it would read a fee, a bill or another debit, never a circular debit or the receiving fee; it still counts for recurrence, and beside a credit with no payout form it holds as any other debit (amended by review round 1 below: it keeps its own kind, marked a card charge, and beside a credit with no payout form holds as that kind does). Pass 6 reads the same. Payment Brands was checked before the move (the orchestrator's condition): it is a card payment facilitator (paymentbrands.com: a registered ISO and member service provider of Fifth Third Bank; its card services agreement, issued through Ministry Brands Holdings with Worldpay as processor, credits the merchant's settlement account with its card transactions and debits it for refunds, chargebacks and processing fees), and on the 8 statements its debits are one fee settlement early each month (0.5% to 4.4% of the month's credits, never at a fixed amount, with a few small fee lines that day) and an occasional chargeback-sized debit, the shape §4.2 describes, never an advance's fixed repayments. Measured: 376 rows, about $5.43 million, leave `payer_also_debited` into True in 27 accounts (Payment Brands' 134 Chase rows, about $5.10 million, and 83 Truist rows, about $0.17 million, two businesses' statements; the 34 descriptor payouts, about $40 thousand, Verified; a field-service platform's 8 rows, about $38 thousand; two practice platforms' 65 and 10 rows, about $37 and $5 thousand; Truist merchant deposits 10 rows, about $25 thousand; RBC's merchant services 17 rows, about $9 thousand, Verified; QuickBooks Payments Canada 6 rows, about $4 thousand, Verified; 5 and 4 smaller processor rows), and 4 sub-dollar rows move to the floor's hold; one CIBC `MISC PAYMENT CANADIAN <association>` credit, held only beside a glued card purchase (its nation word R02's), is counted too; nothing leaves True. 8be3257.
- **The counter-debit holds read names, never a rail, a bank label or a place** (§5.2's person-to-person payer region, §8.1 `payer_debited_as_lender` and `payer_also_debited`, this section's B4 and B5 lines; audit round 2, R2-B02: R02; standard A6, A8, A16): a wallet's cash-out (`VENMO`, `CASH APP`, `SQUARE CASH`, `APPLE CASH` leading the sender: Chase's `FROM: VENMO`, `RTP CREDIT VENMO`, `ELECTRONIC DEPOSIT FROM VENMO`) names no sender, so no wallet debit holds it (amends B5's line above); the rails' own words (`ZELLE`, `INTERAC`, `VENMO`, `POPMONEY`, `AUTODEPOSIT`, `CASH APP`) and RBC's French label `PAIEMENT` are never a payer's or a debit's word, and neither are the place words (the US states, the provinces and territories, `CANADA`, `CANADIAN`, `AMERICAN`) outside the lender hold, which keeps them (a funder may carry a state's name); a person-to-person or push credit from a sender whose name has two words or more matches a debit only on that whole name, its first two words glued, and (two refinements of the brief's wording, so that no rail word newly read as a debit's name holds another payer) a person-to-person or push debit to such a name is matched only on that whole name too, while in the lender hold a sender printing a company form is compared as before (amended by review round 1 below: the whole name is a person's in both holds, one more arm of the lender hold, and a debit for the credit's own amount still matches its first word); on an e-Transfer row whose payer region is empty (RBC's and TD's `e-Transfer received <sender>`, `AUTODEPOSIT <sender>`) the sender is read as Q4 reads it (only there: a wallet's push printing a one-word display name without `FROM` still reads nothing); an ATM, ABM or cash withdrawal that is no circular money never holds a payer (a transfer made at an ATM still does); `Paiement divers COMPTE CHEQUE <n>` or `COMPTE EPARGNE <n>` is held as `transfer_account_designation`. The receiver originators' grouping word and `_counter_debit_skip_words` are unchanged. Measured: held to True, the wallet cash-outs 129 rows, about $192 thousand, in 21 accounts (126 counted `p2p`, about $179 thousand; 3 round sums of one sender move to `p2p_round_repeat`), customers held beside a payment to another person sharing a given name 62 rows, about $40 thousand, in 23 accounts, branch deposits beside a same-town ABM withdrawal 3 rows, about $12 thousand, one state-named customer, `Paiement divers <customer>` beside a lender's `Paiement divers` debit 3 rows, about $7 thousand (the fourth, the holder's own `COMPTE CHEQUE`, is held as `transfer_account_designation`), and one bank-centre credit from the holder's savings that only an ATM withdrawal at its bank held (an unexplained mover: review round 1 below holds it, and its counted twin, as `transfer_account_designation`); True to held, the same-name round trips 362 rows, about $453 thousand, in 154 accounts (355 person-to-person credits, about $215 thousand, among them 49 RBC e-Transfers, about $62 thousand, and 7 incoming wires, about $238 thousand, beside a Zelle to the sender); held to held, 200 round sums (about $253 thousand) and one e-Transfer with an account designation now read `payer_also_debited` first. The round trips exceed the brief's estimate (about 269 rows and $144 thousand, at most 312 rows and $346 thousand): the whole-name match also pairs names printed with a middle initial or a suffix on one side, which the audit's regex scan dropped. 8be3257.
- **The bank's receiving fee reads its outgoing words before the sender it reprints** (§8.1 `payer_also_debited`, this section's B4 line; audit round 2, R2-B02: R48 part 1): `FUNDS TRANSFER FEE TT <sender>` and `WIRE TRANSFER FEE FROM <sender>` read `OUTGOING`, `OUT`, `TO`, `BNF` and `BENEFICIARY` on the fee's label and the words before `TT` or `FROM`, never in the sender's name, so the fee for a wire from `CONTOSO TO GO INC` never holds that sender's repeat wires; a fee for the holder's own outgoing wire (`WIRE TRANSFER FEE TO …`, `WIRE FEE FOR WIRE TRANSFER TO …`, `… DOMESTIC OUTGOING`, `BNF:`) still does, and a bare `WIRE TRANSFER FEE <name>` reads as before (review round 1 below: and `WIRE TRANSFER FEE FROM CHECKING TO …`). Measured: 0 of the corpus's 313 distinct receiving-fee debit texts change kind; 0 rows move. 8be3257.

- **The lender hold reads a person's whole name as one more arm, never the only one** (§8.1 `payer_debited_as_lender`; audit round 2, R2-B02 review round 1: R02; amends 8be3257's lender hold, where a person-to-person or push credit's whole-name key replaced the first-word, B4 and prefix arms for a sender printing no company form): the hold exists so that a funder's money never enters True, so a credit whose whole name (its `person_key`) a lender debit prints is held, and otherwise the credit is compared as before the batch (5.6's first word, one registry entity, the B4 forms, the prefix), the label words skipped and the place words kept: a funder printing no legal form and no financing root that pushes by a real-time, Zelle or Chase real-time rail and pulls under a glued or one-word name (`RTP CREDIT <NAME> VENTURE` beside weekly `<NAME>VENTUREHO` or `<NAME> DES:DEBIT` pulls) is held as `payer_debited_as_lender` again, a reviewable hold even where a person shares a funder's first word. The separate lender key is gone: the whole-name key is a person's in both holds (below). Measured: 0 rows move; `payer_debited_as_lender` stays at 51 rows (the 4 rows the batch moved out of it are RBC's `Paiement divers` rows: 3 customers the label word no longer matches and the holder's own `COMPTE CHEQUE`, held earlier as `transfer_account_designation`). 33fef1c.
- **A company's push and a debit for the credit's own amount are matched as before** (§8.1 `payer_also_debited`; R2-B02 review round 1: R02; amends 8be3257's whole-name match, which replaced every arm for any named person-to-person or push sender): the whole-name key is set only on a sender printing no company form, so a company's push is compared by the B4 forms as before the batch; and a debit for the credit's own amount that is no person-to-person or push debit to a recipient printing two name words still matches the credit's first word (B4: the credit undoes it). Measured: 25 credits in 20 accounts: 4 are held again as before the batch (2 company senders and 2 senders beside a same-amount transfer to the sender's given name, about $6 thousand together); 19 counted before the batch are held now, about $9 thousand: 17 person-to-person credits beside a Zelle or e-Transfer sent for the same amount to the sender's given name alone, and 2 small Zelle credits from a company the holder also pays by Zelle (the same-person round trips of the R02 line above, which now count 374 person-to-person rows, about $224 thousand, in 159 accounts, beside the 7 wires); 2 round sums move from `p2p_round_repeat` to `payer_also_debited`. A Zelle or e-Transfer debit printing a given name and an initial is read by the given name, so one credit from a sender of another surname is held beside such a debit for its own amount (a reviewable hold). 33fef1c.
- **A card charge keeps its kind** (§8.1 `payer_also_debited`; R2-B02 review round 1: R01; amends 8be3257's `card` kind, under which a credit with no payout form was held by a card-form bill as by any other debit): a card purchase the card-form prefix misses is marked a card charge and keeps its kind (a fee, a bill or another debit); the mark only lifts a payout's hold (unless the charge is for the payout's own amount), so beside a credit with no payout form a bill or subscription printed in a card form (`BILL PAY <vendor> <city> <ST> USA`, `<vendor> SUBSCRIPTION <city> <ST> USA`) reads B4's bill rule again, and pass 6 no longer demotes a verified remittance beside one (standard A1: a card purchase is spending). Measured: 0 rows move. 33fef1c.
- **A credit from an account designation with no transfer word is held** (§8.1 and T13 `transfer_account_designation`; R2-B02 review round 1: R02; amends 8be3257's R02 line, under which Bank of America's `BKOFAMERICA BC <date> <ref> FR SAV` entered True once the ATM exclusion dropped the only debit that held it, by the bank's word): a credit printing `FR` before an account word (`SAV`, `SAVINGS`, `SVGS`, `CHKG`, `CHK`, `CKG`, `CHECKING`, `DDA`, `MMA`) names an account, never a payer, and is held as `transfer_account_designation` before the counter-debit holds, whatever debits the statement prints (held for review, as T13 holds a named transfer's account designation: the account may be the holder's own or another customer's). Measured: the corpus's 4 such credits, all Bank of America banking-centre rows: the batch's mover and its twin, counted before the batch, leave True (about $4 thousand together), and 2 held rows (about $14 thousand) change reason from `payer_also_debited`. 33fef1c.
- **The batch's markers read no lookalike** (§8.1; R2-B02 review round 1: R18, R02, R48; amends 8be3257): a payout id holds letters and digits (`ID:ST <town>` and a number alone are none, so a customer's `ID:ST <town>` payment beside its own debit stays held); a company whose name leads with `ATM` or `ABM` (a legal form within its next two words) or an ACH entry printing one is a payee, never a cash withdrawal; in the receiving fee, `FROM` before the holder's own account (an account word or a masked number) or before `TO` starts no sender, so `WIRE TRANSFER FEE FROM CHECKING TO <payee>` is the fee for the holder's own wire again, and `OUTGOING`, `BNF` and `BENEFICIARY`, which no sender's name prints, are read after the sender too. Kept as the brief wrote them and documented (they only lift a one-off other or card hold and never verify; 0 corpus rows): the merchant-ID field read anywhere on the row and the settlement words from a payer that is no processor (a law firm's `DES:SETTLEMENT`); a fee `FROM <holder> TO <payee>` reads as a sender named with `TO` (no hold depends on it: the debit reads the holder's name, never the payee). Measured: 0 rows move; the corpus's 12,474 ATM, ABM and cash debits keep their exclusion and its 3,447 receiving-fee debits keep their kinds. 33fef1c.
- **Audit round 2, R2-B02 review round 1, in all** (8be3257's tree to 33fef1c, the re-parsed basis, 2,513 accounts): True 68.637% → 68.630% (−$18,315.84), Verified $79,862,982.83 (28.696%) unchanged, held 16.558% → 16.564%; 29 credit rows move in 24 statements and True falls in 21 (by at most 9.3 points of a statement's posted credits); `payer_also_debited` 2,571 → 2,594 rows, `payer_debited_as_lender` 51 and `holder_name` 412 unchanged; no verified demotion, no row leaves `non_revenue`, G18 finds no parse move and every checked guard holds; the G7 lender census (re-parsed, against R2-B01's baseline, the owner's allowlist): 0 lenders gained or lost, 0 row-flag moves, eligible deposits unchanged. The batch with its review round (R2-B01's tree to 33fef1c): True 66.755% → 68.630% (+$5,219,463.88), Verified 28.677% → 28.696% (+$53,564.20: 57 rows promoted, none demoted), 1,174 credit rows move in 265 statements, True changes in 236 (75 up, 161 down), statements with $0 True 140 → 142. 33fef1c.
- **A payer region that names only a bank never verifies and names no customer** (§8.6 Q6's eligible rows, §8.10's reasons; audit round 2, R2-B03: R05 part A; amends B2's I01 line above, which read a bank's name as no payer only beside an account description, and §8.6, whose company-form arm alone refused a bank): pass 7 reads a payer region that, once its digit and reference tokens and a trailing `ACH`, `ONLINE` or `N A` are dropped, is only a listed sending bank's name (`P2P_SENDING_BANK_NAMES`), only the statement's own bank's name (its channel: `CHASE ONLINE` on a Chase statement), or one to four words ending `BANK`, `BANCORP`, `FSB` or `CREDIT UNION` that the classifier reads as a bank by its own form (`_bank_only_payer`). Q6 never verifies it, since its repetition is the bank's or the channel's, never a customer's, and it is counted as `no_payer_printed` instead of `named_payer_one_off` or `named_payer_unlabelled` (a verification-only word still names it `named_payer_purpose_refused`). A bank's merchant deposit or card-settlement entry (`DEPOSIT`, `DEP`, `MTOT`, `BTOT`, `SETTLE…`, `STLMT`, `MERCH…`: Bank of America's `DES:DEPOSIT` rows stay Verified), a bank's settlement originator, a company or food bank whose name holds the bank word, and a row printing a person-to-person label (`P2P`: the bank is a person's sending bank, which Q4 reads) keep their readings, as do wires, Q7 and the registry's and descriptors' verifications. The census declares the demotion `q5_q6_bank_only_payer`, read on the pass's own `bank_only_payer` fact. Measured: 36 verified rows leave Verified (about $63 thousand: 28 `CHASE ONLINE … ONLINE PMTSEC CTX` bill payments on Chase statements and 3 `<bank> … SEC:CIE` bill payments are counted, 5 Relay `ACH CREDIT JPMORGAN CHASE` pulls are held by the next line), and 11 counted rows (about $25 thousand) change reason only. 579a907.
- **An ACH pull that names only a bank is held as a transfer with no counterparty** (§8.2 and T13 `transfer_no_counterparty`, Q2; R2-B03: R05 parts B and D; amends B2's I01 line above): an ACH or unlabelled credit whose bank-only payer region is a listed sending bank or a bank by its own form, printed with no entry field, which no rule verified and nothing claimed (the registry, a payout entity, a factoring payee or a factor), is the holder's pull from another of its bank accounts and is held for review (standard B1, B2); the statement's own bank's channel never is (`CHASE ONLINE` stays counted). The registry's sending banks add `MSPBNA` (Morgan Stanley Private Bank) and `ALLY BANK` (Ally Bank), so Bank of America's `MSPBNA DES:ACH TRNSFR` reads no counterparty either. Measured: 14 rows leave True, about $51 thousand, in 10 statements: 7 Relay `ACH CREDIT JPMORGAN CHASE` (5 verified before; one statement loses 86.6% of its True), 4 U.S. Bank `ELECTRONIC DEPOSIT FROM COMMERCE BANK`, 2 `MSPBNA` transfers and 1 Relay `ACH CREDIT ALLY BANK`. Whether each pulled account is the holder's own is inferred (an Ally Bank account cannot be a business's), never proven; the hold is reviewable. 579a907.

A sender reader for these labels (`_WIRE_SENDER_AFTER_LABEL` arms for `INBOUND WIRE`, `INBOUND WIRE|INCOMING FEDWIRE TRANSFER FROM` and `WIRE TRANSFERS? CREDIT`, with bank-originated and holder controls, measured on the corpus) is left for a later batch, beside the owner's I12. Measured: 0 tier or reason moves; only Huntington's 28 `INCOMING FEDWIRE TRANSFER` rows ($698,803.80) become N1 wire rows, still held `wire_no_sender` with no payer region and class `other`. ce7c41e.
- **Zions' footer and Navy Federal's header are statement furniture, and the plural wire label is a wire** (§7.0's revenue text, §6.3 narrowing 11, §7.9 N1, §8.5; audit round 2, R2-B10: R21 parts A, B (the classifier's guard beside R2-B06's parser line), C and D; Q2, Q3, Q5, standard A5, A6; amends B8's I46 lines above): the furniture, in the classifier and in the census's copy, adds Zions' legal-name footer `A DIVISION OF ZIONS BANCORPORATION N A` before a page counter (a wire relayed `VIA <bank> A DIVISION OF ZIONS …` prints none after it and is never cut) and Navy Federal's `[STATEMENT OF ACCOUNT] JOINT OWNER(S)` header, so a bare Zions branch deposit carrying a page break's footer reads `no_payer_printed` (it read `named_payer_unlabelled`, the bank's legal name its payer region) and a Navy Federal deposit or ACH payer carrying the header is no longer held on its `OWNER` (a mobile deposit is a counted check deposit, a payer with a clean twin Verified); no `POS DEBIT` card-credit rule is added. The `WIRE TRANSFER` label reads the plural as a wire label (`WIRE TRANSFERS DEPOSIT|CREDIT|IN|INCOMING|FROM`), never a person-to-person transfer, and the sender reader reads `WIRE TRANSFER[S] IN <sender>` as it reads `INCOMING`, `FROM` and `DEPOSIT`: three round-sum `WIRE TRANSFERS FROM <company>` rows are Verified by Q5 where they were held as `p2p_round_repeat`, and two `WIRE TRANSFER IN <company>` rows Verified where they were held as `wire_no_sender`; a bare `WIRE TRANSFER[S] IN`, a reference after it, `WIRE TRANSFER IN WIRE FROM <name>` and a funder after the label keep their readings, and `TRANSFERS` stays transfer wording on every other row. Part D changes no reader: the cut drops every word after the first form, so a loan, a refund or an own-account transfer printed after a page break inside a row's own description is read as the deposit alone (`DEPOSIT PAGE 2 OF 8 LOAN PROCEEDS` is counted `no_payer_printed`; a test pins it); reading that tail would bring back the furniture's own words (I46), so the census counts the case instead (the census line below). Measured on the re-parsed basis: 3 Zions rows ($34,999.75, one holder's three statements) change reason only, `named_payer_unlabelled` → `no_payer_printed`; Zions' BANKCARD row carrying the footer stays Verified; no Navy Federal credit carries the header after R2-B06's parser fix, so none moves; 0 wire rows move (no corpus credit prints `WIRE TRANSFERS`, and the corpus's `WIRE TRANSFER IN` rows are bare or print `WIRE FROM`). ce7c41e.
- **A funds-transfer wire label that prints only a reference is a wire with no sender** (§7.11's book and funds transfers, §6.3 narrowing 4, §8.5 "Held", R-d's designation; audit round 2, R2-B10: R11; Q5, which outranks §7.11's own-account decision; standard A5): `_own_book_or_funds_transfer` reads a row an incoming-wire label starts that prints no third-party originator and, after its label, no account word and no own-account product word, every word after it a reference, a single letter or a wire label word (the R2-B10 review round 1 line below) (`_bare_incoming_wire`: RBC's `FUNDS TRANSFER CREDIT TT <reference> [O]` and `… TT RBC<reference>`, `INCOMING FUNDS TRANSFER <reference>`, `INBD FUNDS TRANSFER RECEIVED <reference>`, `FUNDS TRANSFER WIRE FROM <reference>`) as no own-account transfer, so the strong rule no longer makes it `non_revenue` and pass 7 holds it as `wire_no_sender`, which a reviewer can confirm and the review gate reads; `prints_internal_transfer_designation` reads the same rule, so the row designates no own account for R-d. No new reason. Unchanged: the non-revenue patterns run first; the holder named as the originator, an account or product word after the label (`TT SAVINGS <n>`, `FROM BUSINESS SAVINGS <n>`, `<n> LOAN PROCEEDS`), a funds transfer no incoming-wire label starts, the `BOOK TRANSFER` branch (T19) and the EFT channel keep the own-account reading, and a registry funder after the label stays financing proceeds. Unconfirmed: what RBC's trailing `O` stands for; if such a TT is the holder's own money from abroad it is now held where it was non-revenue, out of True either way, and a reviewer who confirms it counts it. Measured: 6 RBC rows, $176,304.47, in 4 statements, move from `non_revenue` to held `wire_no_sender`; True and Verified are unchanged to the cent; the review gate turns on for 2 statements; all 4 are single-account documents, so no R-d pair changes. ce7c41e.
- **A loose-wire originator label is read only after the row's first `WIRE` word** (§8.5's wire family, §5.2's wire payer region; audit round 2, R2-B10: R26; Q5, standard A5, B1, B7, C1; amends B5's I14 line above): `_loose_wire_label` reads the first `ORG`, `ORIG`, `ORIGINATOR` or `B O` after the row's first `WIRE` word, searched from that position so its left boundary is still read, and the payer region reads the sender from that label (`_wire_payer_region(text, start)`); a label word before `WIRE` is a word of the sender's own name on the `<sender> WIRE` layout (Mercury, Brex). So a holder whose name prints `ORG`, `ORIG` or `ORIGINATOR` wiring its own money is held `holder_name` (it was counted), `<name> ORG WIRE` and a `.org` domain are counted `wire_named_sender` (they were held `wire_no_sender`), and a funder whose financing word precedes the label word is held `financing_named_payer` (it was counted); `<name> ORG DEPOSIT WIRE IN <ref> ORG <sender>` still reads the sender after its own label. Unchanged: N1 rows (Columbia's `WIRE DEPOSIT WIRE IN`, U.S. Bank's `WIRE CREDIT REF … ORG`, Santander), both label patterns, the registry and non-revenue precedence, the callers of `_loose_wire_originator`; the older cut of an `ORG` inside a name on an N1 row stays out of scope. Residual: a loose layout that printed its label before `WIRE` would lose its sender (none in the corpus: every loose credit prints `B O` after `WIRE`). Measured: 0 rows; over the B12 walk's real rows the loose arm reads the same 12 credits and 80 debits before and after, with 0 payer regions changed (4 credits on the census basis). ce7c41e.

- **A bare incoming wire prints only a reference, read word by word** (§7.11's book and funds transfers, §6.3 narrowing 4, §8.5 "Held"; audit round 2, R2-B10 review round 1: R11; Q5; amends the R11 line above, whose reader dropped only account and product words, so a funds-transfer label's row printing a reference and a holder-noise, generic, currency or legal-form word, `FUNDS TRANSFER CREDIT TT <n> BUSINESS|TRUST|USD|PAYMENT|ONLINE|INC|THE`, `INBD FUNDS TRANSFER RECEIVED <n> USD` or `FUNDS TRANSFER WIRE FROM <n> USD`, left the own-account reading only to be counted as `wire_named_sender` with that word as its "sender", the opposite of the ruling's direction): `_bare_incoming_wire` is true only when every word after the label is a reference token (digits, a masked token, a month), a single letter (RBC's `O` or `W`) or a wire label word (`_BARE_WIRE_LABEL_WORDS`: §8.5's one label list and the funds-transfer labels' `TT` and `FROM`, a test keeping it equal to `true_revenue._WIRE_LABEL_ONLY_WORDS` plus those two); any other word keeps the own-account reading (`non_revenue`, an own-account designation for R-d), as before R11; and `credit_facts` blanks the payer region of a row `_bare_incoming_wire` reads, so a month or a stray `TT`, `FROM` or `WIRE FROM <n>` the payer reader keeps is no sender and the row is held `wire_no_sender`, never counted. The reader's name now means what it says: on the B12 walk's real rows it is false on 2,449 rows it read as bare before (N1 rows printing a sender the A3 reader or an originator field reads, and fee and outgoing debits), none of which changes a reading (0 designation flips, 0 payer-region changes). Recorded residual: a numbered company printed after a funds-transfer label (`FUNDS TRANSFER CREDIT TT <n> ONTARIO INC`) keeps the own-account reading it had before R11, because the funds-transfer originator reader drops its words; reading it as a counted sender (OQ3) belongs to that reader, not to this one; no corpus row prints one (no real funds-transfer-label credit of the B12 walk keeps the own-account reading with no originator), and a test pins the reading (ea8d19f). Measured on the re-parsed basis, R2-B10's tree to this one: 0 credit rows move; True $189,523,694.57 (68.099%), Verified $79,733,229.52 (28.649%) and held $47,524,373.27 (17.076%) unchanged; against R2-B09 review round 1's tree the same 9 rows move as the batch line above says (R11's 6 RBC rows under `13_bare_incoming_wire`, R21's 3 Zions rows by reason), row for row; G18 finds no parse move, eligible deposits and lender credits are unchanged on every account, the furniture tail reads 0 and every checked guard holds; G7 (re-parsed, against the batch's lender census, the owner's allowlist): 0 lenders gained or lost, 0 row-flag moves, 0 outside the allowlist. Cached basis: 0 rows move against the batch's tree, and the same 9 against R2-B09 review round 1's. No parser change, no analysis stage bump, `CLASSIFICATION_VERSION` stays `"5.6"`. 61ec89f.
